- **Two real RLS visibility gaps found and fixed, live co-testing as a head coach and an assistant on the same practice** (forty-seventh session): `assets_select_access` and `locations_select_access` were both, per the original code comment, "identical shape" -- `can_access_owned` only, with no branch at all for "this row is used by a practice/station on a team I coach." A personally-owned (non-org) asset or location was completely invisible under RLS to any *other* coach on the same team, even one fully authorized to run that exact practice. This silently broke, for that second coach specifically: the whole Equipment Needed section and every per-drill/per-station equipment pill (assets resolve to nothing, `equipNamesFor` filters them out), and everything location-dependent -- the practice's own location name, every Area shown against a drill/station, Equipment Needed's area grouping, and the transition screen's area summary (all fell back to their "no location" paths, which is why the transition screen read "Station 1 to Station 2" for the assistant instead of the real area names the head coach saw for the *same* practice). Fixed at the function level, not just the raw policies, so dependents fix transitively: `can_access_location` (called by both `locations_select_access` and, unchanged, `sublocations_select_access`) now also returns true when the location is referenced by a practice on a team the caller can access (`20260813161000_locations_practice_visibility.sql`); `assets_select_access` got two new `exists` branches for the same shape, keyed off `practice_activity_equipment`/`station_equipment`'s own already-correct `can_access_practice_activity`/`can_access_station` checks (`20260813160000_assets_practice_station_visibility.sql`). Verified both via the assistant's own real REST token before and after each migration (empty result -> the real row).
- **Practice Setup's present/absent toggles, and "I'm here" coach presence, now actually sync live across every coach viewing it -- real bug found live co-testing, previously pure local `useState`, never written anywhere** (forty-seventh session): two new array columns on `practice_live_sessions`, `setup_present_player_ids`/`setup_present_coach_ids` (`20260813162000_setup_stage_attendance_sync.sql`) -- an ephemeral in-progress draft, deliberately not the append-only `session_attendance` table (that one's the real historical record, committed once via `submitAttendanceSnapshot` only when "Run Practice" is actually tapped). Living on the session row means every toggle rides the exact sync machinery every other live-session field already uses (realtime, the reconcile poll, `HelperView`'s poll) with no new sync code needed -- just new columns to sync. `start_or_join_live_session` now seeds `setup_present_player_ids` server-side at creation (the whole roster minus anyone with a `planned_absences` row for this practice -- a single source of truth from the first moment the row exists, replacing the old client-side `freshPresent` computation duplicated on every viewer's own mount) and marks the creating coach present as a coach immediately; any *later* coach reaching Setup while it's still unconfirmed gets appended to `setup_present_coach_ids` too if they're not in it yet -- "a coach lands on Setup and is marked present by default" now actually persists and syncs, instead of being local-only state nobody else ever saw. **A genuine two-writer race avoided, not just papered over**: a per-tap toggle deliberately does *not* go through the normal `writeSession` version-checked read-modify-write -- two real coaches each computing "add/remove one id from the array I last saw" from their own possibly-a-beat-stale local snapshot is a genuine concurrent-edit case (not the single-client rapid-double-tap `writeQueueRef` already handles), and a version conflict there doesn't merge two edits, it silently discards whichever one lost -- exactly the "why didn't the other coach's screen update" symptom being fixed, just relocated. `toggle_setup_presence(session_id,kind,target_id)` (`20260813163000_toggle_setup_presence.sql`) sidesteps the read-modify-write shape entirely: one atomic UPDATE, add-or-remove decided from the row's own current value at write time under Postgres' normal row-level locking, so two concurrent toggles of two *different* ids can never clobber each other regardless of which snapshot either client started from. Bulk actions (Mark All Present / Clear All) still go through the normal `writeSession` path -- rarer, deliberate, whole-array replacements where "the loser just re-taps" is an acceptable, visible fallback, unlike a single silently-dropped tap. Verified live: toggled a player present from one identity, confirmed it appeared on the other coach's already-open screen within a couple seconds with no reload.
- **A coach's own Practice Setup screen now automatically moves into the live view the instant *any* coach taps Run Practice, instead of only the tab that tapped it -- real bug found live co-testing**: the mount effect that resolves `stage` ("attend" vs "live") only ever ran once, at mount, from whatever `setup_confirmed_at` looked like *then* -- session state itself already synced live across every viewer, but nothing was reactively watching for `setup_confirmed_at` flipping true *after* mount and catching a given tab's own local `stage` up to it, so every other coach had to notice and tap Run Practice themselves too. Fixed with a new effect (`CommandScreen.jsx`) that reacts to the session's already-synced fields directly -- moves `stage` forward only (`attend`->`live`, ->`end`), and is a no-op for whichever tab's own click already advanced its own `stage` synchronously first. Verified live: flipped `setup_confirmed_at` on the session row from outside the coach's own open tab and watched it jump straight from Practice Setup into the live "Introduce Stations" view within a couple seconds, unattended.
- **Coach-name pills, made consistent for every viewer, not just whoever currently holds control** (forty-seventh session, direct feedback: "the head coach's view has the coach name for each drill in a pill but the assistant shows the full name without a pill"): `SetupActivityRow`/`SetupStationBlockRow` (Practice Setup) used to render `LeaderTapTarget` (a real pill) only when `isController`, falling back to a bare, unstyled `<span>` otherwise -- same component now renders unconditionally for both, with `disabled` (not a second, differently-styled branch) controlling whether it's tappable. `LeaderTapTarget` itself, and the plain-activity leader line on the main live screen (both the coach view and `HelperView`, previously a bare colored `<span>` with no pill at all), all now read "Coach: {name}" (or "Coach: Unassigned") consistently. **The standalone "COACH" label that used to sit in front of the pill on Practice Setup's own rows was removed same day, direct feedback it was now redundant with the pill's own "Coach:" prefix.** Deliberately left alone: the underlined dotted-tap-target coach-reassignment links deeper in the live rotation/station-focus views (`leadName(st)` sites) -- a different, pre-existing "tap to edit" affordance, not the informational pill this feedback was about.
- **Up Next, inside a station block, now shows what's actually next once the whole block finishes, not a vague "Station rotation coming up" placeholder** (forty-seventh session continued, direct feedback: "it should show what is next after the station block, not the next station rotation"). `liveActs.slice(idx+1)` (coach view) and `session.upcoming_activities` (`HelperView`) already meant "everything after the current *top-level* activity" regardless of which rotation is showing -- the mid-block special case (and its "See next transition" local-only preview, now fully removed as dead code along with its `previewTrans` state/modal) was hiding that real answer behind a placeholder instead of just showing it. Also now shows every remaining activity, scrollable, instead of capping at 1-3 -- both the coach's own live view and `HelperView` (`get_live_session_view`'s own `upcoming_activities` field was already uncapped server-side; only the client-side slice was hiding the rest) render the full remaining list inside a `maxHeight:220,overflowY:"auto"` scroll container.
- **Equipment and player gear are shown as two separately-labeled pill groups everywhere the live view displays either, not one merged list** (forty-seventh session continued, second pass): `EquipGearRow`/`PlayerGearPill` (amber "Equipment" / orange "Player Gear", both acquired-aware) across the top-of-live plain-drill badge, Introduce Stations' block-intro, the per-rotation focused-station view, and the all-stations tap list, in both the coach view and `HelperView`. `get_live_session_view` returns `equipment`/`player_gear` as two typed `{name,acquired}` arrays (`20260814000000_live_session_equipment_split_by_type.sql`); the coach view splits its own local `data.assets`-resolved ids by `.type` client-side, no RPC involved. Introduce Stations' equipment badge (forty-seventh session, first pass) is now solid-amber on every one of these sites, not just `HelperView` -- the first pass's fix used a search string that only matched `HelperView`'s own local variable name, silently missing the coach's identically-styled but differently-named one.
- **Block-intro ("Introduce Stations") auto-advances into Rotation 1 on its own once its own countdown reaches zero, no buzzer, no manual Next** (forty-seventh session continued, second pass) -- same shape as the existing transition-screen auto-advance, `isController`-gated, confirmed synced to a passive `HelperView` tab with no action taken there.
- **The transition-screen rotation summary is now bold and noticeably larger** (forty-seventh session, direct feedback: "increase the text size and bold it so it stands out for them to quickly find and read to the team") -- 13px/`#ccc` to 17px/800-weight/white, both views.
- **The station-block name (added forty-sixth session) now also prefixes `HelperView`'s own block-stats line**, matching the coach view's own "{name} · N Stations · Mmin total" -- previously coach-view-only.

### Notes (Assistant Coach handoff §2)
- Two grains: per-drill/station (`practice_activity_id`/`station_id` set) and end-of-practice (both null). Backed by the `notes` table (existed since 2026-07-14 for the staff-facing capture UI above) plus a new `note_player_tags` join table for @mentions.
- **Anonymous helper composer reworded** (seventeenth session): the "End-of-practice note" checkbox (checked = general, unchecked = tagged to the current drill) is now a two-option toggle showing the actual choice — the current drill's name vs. "General" — same underlying `endOfPractice` boolean and data model, just a clearer control than a checkbox whose two states didn't read as a real choice.
- **Unreviewed-note red dot** (seventeenth session): `notes.viewed_at` (nullable, `20260730020000_note_review_tracking.sql`) plus a `has_unviewed_notes` flag added to `get_team_session_history`. Goals & Insights' History list shows a red dot per session with any non-archived, unviewed note; opening that session's detail as the head coach marks its notes viewed automatically (gated on `canManage` specifically, not any viewer, so an assistant/helper glancing at read-only history doesn't silently clear the head coach's unread indicator); a "Mark all as viewed" action clears every visible session at once. Marking is a plain client-side update against `viewed_at`, covered by the same `notes_update_archive` RLS policy `archiveNote` already uses — no new RPC needed for either the single-practice or whole-team case.
- **Anonymous helpers can now write notes too** — a first for this app; every other anonymous surface (`/preview/:token`, `/live/:token`) is read-only by design. Token-validated server-side (`submit_practice_note_by_token`, reuses the existing `validate_token()` — never a client-asserted author), with a flat 40-notes-per-token rate limit and a 500-character cap (both confirmed defaults, not assumed). `HelperView` got a new "Notes" panel (there was no note UI there at all before) with the same @mention picker, using the same minimized roster (first name + last initial + jersey) helpers already see everywhere else, plus an optional freeform name field (`author_label` — display only, never trusted as identity).
- Archive-don't-delete: `notes` had no UPDATE policy at all before this session (comment called it "quick-capture-only") — added one, gated the same as read/insert (`can_access_practice`).
- Surfaced in `SessionHistoryDetail` (Goals & Insights, grouped by drill/end-of-practice, author+role shown, tagged players as chips) and, new, on `PlayerProfile` ("Practice Notes" section, reverse-chronological across every practice this player was ever @mentioned in, via `fetchNotesForPlayer`).

### Helper/parent-facing (anonymous, token-based, no login)
- `/preview/:token` — pre-practice setup view (equipment, roster, countdown to start).
- `/live/:token` — live view during a run (current drill/station, roster, optional attendance-marking scope), now also a note-writing surface (see Notes above) — the first anonymous write path in this app. For a group/station drill, each player is shown as a tappable chip; tapping reveals that player's per-category focus note (if the coach has set one) alongside the drill's own skill tags — resolved server-side inside `get_live_session_view` (tag names via `library_activity_id → drill_tags → skill_tags`, notes via that tag's `category_id → player_focus_areas`, team-scoped), since the anonymous helper has no client-side access to `activityLibrary`/`skillTags` the way the staff view does.
- One-tap **Practice Setup View** on Home's next-practice hero card, for any signed-in coach (head coach or assistant): finds/creates a preview token for that practice and opens the same `/preview/:token` page a shared link would, reusing the identical component/query rather than a second one. Always visible once a plan exists, deliberately not time-gated the way the anonymous link is — that gating exists only because the anon link has no auth, which doesn't apply to a signed-in staff member.
- Player data is minimized (first name + last-initial + jersey), everything else (equipment, coaching points, location) is shown in full — a deliberate privacy choice, not an oversight.

### Public landing page, feedback, and legal
- `/` is adaptive on session state: signed-out renders `LandingPage`, signed-in renders the app — deliberately not a hard route split, so an installed PWA icon (`start_url: '/'`) keeps launching straight into the app.
- Landing page is a full 14-section product-walkthrough marketing site with real app CSS-class-built visuals (not screenshots, not stylized mockups) using realistic sample data — pixel-consistent with the actual UI by construction.
- `/terms`, `/privacy` render final, attorney-cleared copy via a single catch-all `vercel.json` rewrite.
- In-app feedback is authenticated only (Home "?" → "Send Feedback"). The landing page's own anonymous feedback form was removed; `submitPublicFeedback`/`feedback` table were kept in place, unused, in case a future non-authenticated contact path wanted them -- **no longer dead code as of the twentieth session**: the Settings/FAQ "Request a Consultation" form (`ConsultationRequestForm`, `LegalPages.jsx`) reuses both `submitFeedback` (authenticated) and `submitPublicFeedback` (anonymous), exactly the future contact path this was speculatively kept around for.
- **A submitted consultation request now actually notifies Jax by email (forty-fourth session)** — asked directly "where does that go?" and found it landed in `feedback` with no alert at all (confirmed live: a real, previously-unnoticed lead was sitting there). A new `pg_net` trigger (`on_feedback_consultation_created_notify`, `20260810010000_consultation_request_notify_trigger.sql`) fires only on rows whose `message` starts with the literal "Organization consultation request" marker `ConsultationRequestForm` writes (not plain Home "Send Feedback" submissions), calling a new `notify-consultation-request` Edge Function (deployed `--no-verify-jwt`) that emails `jaxon@runofpractice.com` via Resend with `reply_to` set to the lead's own contact email, using a plain internal-notification template, not the coach-facing `renderEmailHtml` sign-in-CTA one. Verified against a real, already-existing feedback row via direct `curl`.

---


## Database & permissions (architecture, not a live snapshot)

Point-in-time row counts and table counts age out fast on a system under active development — the last full live-DB introspection was 2026-07-21, and several sessions have touched the schema since (the org-model rework and Coach/Org mode work alone add and drop tables/columns). What's stable and worth keeping here is the **architecture**, not a specific count. Run a fresh introspection against the live project before trusting any specific number.

**Ownership patterns**
- **Team-scoped**: practices, stations, session data — access via `can_access_team`/`can_manage_team`, which check `teams.owner_user_id`, org membership (`teams.organization_id` + `is_org_member`/`is_org_admin`), or a non-archived `team_staff` row.
- **Coach-or-org ("owned")**: drills, assets, locations, templates, skill tags — `owner_user_id` XOR `organization_id`, checked via `can_access_owned`/`can_manage_owned` (the latter requires org-admin, not just org-member, for org-owned rows — keeps shared libraries curated).
- **Anonymous/token tier**: `session_access_tokens` (scope, expiry, revocation) gates `/preview/:token` and `/live/:token` entirely through `SECURITY DEFINER` RPCs — `anon` has no direct table grants beyond `REFERENCES/TRIGGER/TRUNCATE` on real tables. As of this session this tier can **write**, not just read, for the first time (`submit_practice_note_by_token`) — same pattern (token validated inside the function, `anon` still has zero direct grants on `notes`/`note_player_tags`).
- **Platform admin**: `admin_users`/`is_admin()` is a flat, separate superadmin gate (founder metrics, public-catalog curation rights) — unrelated to org roles.

**RLS conventions used everywhere**
- Almost no DELETE policy anywhere — archive via `archived_at`, never hard-delete through the app. Exceptions: join tables where removing an association isn't destroying history, and one explicit audit-adjustment delete policy on `session_activity_log`.
- Actor-identity columns (`created_by`, `marked_by`, `noted_by`, etc.) are enforced via `WITH CHECK (col = auth.uid())`, never trusted from the client.
- `service_role` bypasses RLS entirely but **not** base-table GRANTs — every new Edge Function or import script that queries app tables directly (rather than through an RPC, which runs as the function owner) needs its own explicit grant. This has bitten more than one session (first `profiles`, later `org_invites`/`organizations`) — check for it early if a new Edge Function mysteriously returns empty/errors.
- `practice_activities`/`template_activities` are full copies of the source drill's fields at add-time, never live references — editing a library drill or template later never retroactively changes anything already built from it. Same lineage-not-binding pattern applies to `templates.default_team_id` and `practices.template_id`.

**Legacy POC tables — dropped 2026-07-27.** `app_data`, `coaches`, and `live_sessions` (the old table) predated the current migration history entirely (created for the pre-rewrite POC, before Postgres RLS/auth existed in this app) and had carried their original wide-open `anon`-era RLS the whole time. Once `app_data`'s last client-side reference was traced and removed (see Session Log), all three were confirmed zero-reference in `src/`, and dropped via `20260727000000_drop_legacy_poc_tables.sql`. If you're looking for them, they're gone on purpose — see Session Log for the verification trail before dropping.

## Known gaps / deliberately deferred (do not build without checking first)

- Hard/GDPR-style account deletion — still not started as an actual feature (the FAQ tells coaches to email support), but several real pieces of the "FK-cascade ordering issue" this entry used to gesture at vaguely are now confirmed and fixed: `practice_series.created_by`, `organizations.created_by`, `feedback.user_id`, `practice_live_sessions`' actor columns (all earlier sessions, all the nullable + `ON DELETE SET NULL` actor-identity pattern), and — twenty-sixth session, found live while tearing down that session's own disposable test account — `activity_library`'s four lineage-pointer FKs (`practice_activities.library_activity_id`, `stations.library_activity_id`, `template_activities.library_activity_id`, `template_stations.library_activity_id`, all previously `no action`; fixed to `on delete set null` in `20260802050000_library_lineage_fk_actor_pattern.sql`, since all four are copy-at-add-time lineage pointers, not live content). **A new instance found the same session, not yet fixed, and deliberately left for a real product decision rather than a unilateral guess**: `session_attendance.player_id references players(id)` also has no `ON DELETE` action, so deleting a player (which cascades from deleting their team, which cascades from deleting their account) blocks on any session they were ever marked present/absent/left-early in. Unlike the lineage-pointer case, `SET NULL` isn't obviously correct here — `session_attendance` is itself the historical record (append-only, "current status = the most recent row"), and `get_team_session_history`'s own attendance count uses `distinct on (sa.player_id)`, which would silently collapse multiple different deleted players' rows into a single NULL group and undercount a session's attendance. Needs a real decision (cascade-delete the attendance rows entirely vs. some anonymized-but-still-counted representation) before fixing, not just an FK migration. Other tables may still have the same gap unaudited; every instance found so far was found by hitting it, not by a systematic sweep — worth an actual audit before this becomes a real self-service feature. **Hit again, thirty-first session, tearing down that session's own test account**: neither `authenticated` nor `service_role` has a direct `DELETE` grant on `session_attendance` at all (`permission denied for table session_attendance`, even via the service-role key) — this table isn't just missing the FK action, it has no delete path through PostgREST for any role. The only way through was `supabase db query --linked --file <path>` (the Management-API SQL path already used for dry-running migrations), which runs with enough privilege to bypass table grants entirely; a plain service-role JS client cannot clean this up. **A third, distinct instance found forty-first session, tearing down that session's own disposable account**: `planned_absences.noted_by references profiles(id)` also has no `ON DELETE` action — marking any player absent (e.g. via `AbsencePicker`) and then trying to delete that coach's account blocks with `update or delete on table "profiles" violates foreign key constraint "planned_absences_noted_by_fkey"`. Unlike `session_attendance`, this one probably *is* just the ordinary actor-identity gap (`noted_by` records who flagged the absence, not the historical fact itself, which is the practice/player pairing) — likely a straightforward `on delete set null` fix, same pattern as `session_attendance.marked_by`/`session_activity_log.logged_by`/`practice_live_sessions.controller_user_id` already got in `20260707190000_live_session_actor_set_null.sql` — but not fixed yet this session, just found and worked around via the same `db query --linked` manual-delete path. Confirms the "other tables may still have the same gap unaudited" line above was right; still worth a real sweep before self-service account deletion ships. **A fourth instance, forty-third session, tearing down that session's own disposable team rather than an account**: `practices.team_id references teams(id)` also has no `ON DELETE` action -- deleting a team while any practice still references it (including old ones from a "Run as New" test, easy to forget about) is silently blocked, not cascaded, and the client-side error surfaced through `admin.auth.admin.deleteUser` as an opaque 500 with no useful message at all. Unlike the three actor-identity cases above, this one isn't about *who did something* -- it's the practice's own real parent -- so `SET NULL` is very unlikely to be the right fix (a practice with no team makes little sense); `ON DELETE CASCADE` is the more plausible direction, but deserves the same real decision (and the same audit of `practices`' own children -- `practice_activities`, `practice_live_sessions`, etc.) before changing it, not a unilateral guess. Worked around this session via the same `db query --linked` manual-delete-in-dependency-order path already established for the other three.
- Week-view / drag-to-reschedule, games/events, series-level default templates, moving a plan between practices, availability polling — explicitly out of scope for now.
- Any other parent/player-facing surface beyond the existing anonymous helper links — explicit product stance: "this is a coach tool, not TeamSnap."
- Push notifications/reminders.
- **A real portrait-only lock for the live view.** Tried forty-fifth session (2026-08-10) after direct feedback that the screen should never rotate to landscape: `manifest.json`'s `"orientation":"portrait"` (an Android/Chrome-installed-PWA-only hint, no effect on iOS or a plain browser tab) plus a pure-CSS full-screen "rotate back to portrait" block in `index.html` for everyone else. Reverted the same day, in full, after direct feedback that the block screen itself was "useless" -- on iOS specifically (where the device genuinely can't be forced back to portrait, only the app hidden until the coach rotates it themselves) it replaced an awkward-but-usable landscape layout with a dead one, and rather than ship an Android-only partial version, the whole thing was pulled back out for everyone until there's a real design for it. **Worth knowing before trying again**: iOS Safari, in a browser tab or installed to the home screen, has never implemented the manifest orientation member or the Screen Orientation lock API, so there is no way to *actually* force an iPhone back to portrait from web code -- any future attempt needs to either accept "block the UI until the coach rotates manually" (what was tried and rejected) as the only real option on iOS, or design the live view to genuinely work in both orientations rather than fighting the platform. Android (manifest orientation, honored for an installed PWA) is a smaller, still-open, lower-priority piece of the same gap.
- A second org role beyond `director` — proposed as `admin` in the original design, but `org_staff.role`'s CHECK constraint only allows `'director'` today, so it doesn't exist as a usable value yet. No billing model exists to define what a second tier would even gate. (An earlier, since-replaced org model had `coach`/`viewer` roles too — see Session Log's "Sharing & org model" — but those aren't part of today's `org_staff` table at all, not just unenforced.)
- Automated test suite — every feature is verified empirically in a live browser against production with disposable Supabase test accounts, by design (no local Supabase environment in this setup).
- ~~Dropping the legacy `app_data`/`coaches`/old `live_sessions` POC tables~~ **Done 2026-07-27** — see Session Log.

- ~~Equipment visibility for a peer-shared drill~~ **Fixed, same day** — `assets_select_access` extended with the same `is_library_peer`/`is_private` branch already used for drill visibility (`20260801090000_assets_peer_sharing_rls.sql`). `can_link_asset_to_practice_activity`'s own comment about this class of limitation for personal-team co-coaches is now stale for the specific peer-sharing case; it may still apply to a plain (non-Permissions-linked) co-coach scenario, not re-verified.
- ~~Two equipment-mismatch dialogs~~ **Done, twenty-fifth session continued** — see Session Log. Now fully closes the loop for every sharing source (org, Public Library, and peer), following the `assets_select_access` fix above.
- Pro+/plan-based gating on who can be granted "Share Practice Planning" — the delegation mechanism itself (twenty-fifth session) is real and RLS-enforced, but whether granting it requires the assistant to be Pro/org-affiliated (unless the head coach has Pro+) is not wired to anything, consistent with `src/entitlements.js` staying fully inert until real billing exists.
- A real, app-writable "add a skill category" flow — `skill_categories` is service-role/SQL-editor seeded only. 6 of the app's 12 sports have categories today (Baseball, Basketball, Football, Soccer, Lacrosse, Volleyball); Softball, Hockey, Tennis, and Swimming have none — no proposal or seed exists for them, this just hasn't been done yet.
- ~~Baseball/Basketball skill-tag duplication~~ **Fixed 2026-07-27** — see Session Log.
- Anonymous helpers can tag a note to a drill (`practice_activity_id`) but not a specific station within a station block (`station_id` is accepted by `submit_practice_note_by_token` but `HelperView`'s composer never passes one) — deliberately kept simple for v1, not a hard limitation of the schema.
- `PlayerProfile`'s new "Practice Notes" section shows the note text/author/date but not which practice it came from — fine for now with one team's worth of history, would want a practice reference once there's enough volume that "which practice was this" becomes a real question.
- `HelperView`'s notes panel is write-only by design (matches the confirmed scope: staff-only screens are where notes get read back) — a helper gets a "Note added" confirmation flash, never a list of what they've submitted.
- **Replace `speechSynthesis` with recorded human audio for the live-practice voice cues** — every synthetic voice tried (a name-based Male/Female heuristic, then the full device voice list) fell short; Jax is arranging real recordings of the four fixed phrases the app ever speaks (`"Two minutes remaining."`, `"Time"`, `"Next up!"`, `"Audio on"`) to send as `.wav`/`.mp3` files. Once received: add them as static files (same pattern as `whistle.wav` etc.), likely as one or more new selectable "voice" options alongside/replacing the current curated Samantha/Daniel `speechSynthesis` picker (`LivePracticeAudioSection`, `SettingsScreen.jsx`) — probably means `beep()`/`speak()` in `CommandScreen.jsx` branching on whether the chosen "voice" is a recorded-clip set or a `speechSynthesis` voice, since a recorded clip can't take arbitrary text the way TTS can (fine here, since only ever these 4 fixed phrases are spoken).
- **Migrate transactional email off Resend's free tier to Google Workspace's SMTP relay** — not urgent, but worth doing soon. Confirmed (research + a second check against Jax's actual account): included at no extra cost on the paid Workspace plan already in use, official per-user limit 10,000/day vs. Resend free tier's ~100/day. One real catch: **new Workspace tenants are capped below that full limit until $100 of billing spend and 60 days of account tenure have passed** (Google's standard anti-abuse ramp-up for new accounts, not anything specific to this setup) — but even under that ramp-up cap, it's still roughly 5x Resend's current ceiling, so it's a strict improvement even before the account "matures." Two separate integration points, not one: Supabase Auth's SMTP setting (dashboard-only, no CLI/API access from this environment) and the two Edge Functions that call Resend's API directly (`notify-team-staff-added`, `notify-org-invite` — code Claude Code *can* edit once Jax has SMTP-relay credentials to hand off via `supabase secrets set`). Use "Require SMTP Authentication" in the relay setup, not IP allowlisting — Supabase's outbound sending IPs aren't static/publishable.
- ~~`org_create_team` still auto-adds the creating director as `team_staff` head_coach~~ **Fixed (Phase 2 follow-up)** — see Session Log and the Organizations section above.

Multi-org library *switching* specifically reuses Home's existing org picker (no new UI needed) rather than being a separate feature, per the decision that scoped this pass.
- ~~Org mode silently reverts to Coach mode on a fresh full page load~~ **Fixed (Phase 2 continued)** — see Organizations section above.

See Live sessions and Gotchas.

Both render at desktop widths no worse than before this pass, but neither got any BB-specific arrangement. A dedicated BB/cast-display pass can come later.

## Working conventions

- No client-side insert loops for bulk operations — always one atomic, server-capped RPC (`create_practice_series` is the model to copy).
- "Archive, don't delete" (`archived_at`) almost everywhere; see RLS conventions above for the exceptions.
- Migrations are always dry-run (`BEGIN;...;ROLLBACK;`) before applying for real, and applied via `npx supabase db push` (the CLI is linked in this environment). If a migration is ever applied via the raw Management API instead, run `migration repair --status applied` before the next `db push --dry-run`, or the CLI will misjudge it as pending.
- Testing pattern for anything requiring a signed-in user: a disposable Supabase test account via the Admin API's `generate_link` + `verifyEmailOtp`, verify via direct SQL, delete after. Never test destructive flows on the real coach's account. Note: `generate_link` and the app's own "Send Code" share a rate-limit bucket.
- **Two persistent QA accounts now exist and should be reused, not deleted (forty-fourth session)**: `ropqa-head@example.com` (head coach, user id `773ae5a8-bbe6-4936-a24b-05606bd8d2e0`) and `ropqa-asst@example.com` (assistant, user id `9cff9d68-fc32-44cd-8257-584d4f650771`), both already on a real team ("QA Persistent Wolves," id `418fe755-4e53-4365-a2da-dd3ecd5cc664`, with players, equipment, and a drill with a deliberately-unowned equipment item already on it, `activity_library.id` `507f8e3c-0153-4458-a615-8fd06d835b0d`). `scripts/qa_login.mjs` mints a fresh session for either account any time (auth sessions expire; the underlying data doesn't) -- run it, inject the returned `access_token`/`refresh_token` into `localStorage` under `sb-bepoojcbizxhqadrytjq-auth-token` the same way every other session in this file's history has. Reuse these two for anything that just needs *a* signed-in head coach/assistant pair; only mint-and-delete a fresh disposable account for something that specifically needs a brand-new signup flow, an empty/zero-state account, or a destructive action (account deletion, etc.) that would ruin these two for next time. Feel free to add more teams/practices/drills to them as future sessions need richer fixtures -- just don't delete the accounts themselves or the "QA Persistent Wolves" team.
- Sign-in email rate limit: `auth.rate_limit.email_sent` in `supabase/config.toml`, currently 30/hour — this is the limit that specifically governs custom-SMTP-sent emails (its own comment says so), not something Resend bypasses. The real ceiling in practice is likely Resend's own account-level plan cap (the project is on Resend's free tier per a code comment in `_shared/emailTemplate.ts`), since that applies across sign-in codes *and* the invite-notification Edge Functions combined — check the Resend dashboard directly for the current number rather than trusting either Supabase setting as authoritative.
- Timezone: always compute/display via the team's own `timezone` column, through `scheduledAtToTeamLocal`/`teamLocalToScheduledAt` — never the viewing device's zone, never bare UTC truncation (`localDateStr()` exists specifically to avoid the UTC-rollover bug described in Session Log).
- Secrets never go into migration files (git-tracked) — Vault or Edge Function secrets only. A `pg_net`-triggered function needs `verify_jwt:false` plus a shared-secret header.
- `.upsert()` still needs an UPDATE grant even for `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` — use `{onConflict, ignoreDuplicates:true}` when a table's RLS is insert/delete-only by design.
- **Every UI change must account for both layouts (forty-ninth session).** The app renders two layouts from one codebase: the mobile-optimized layout (below 1024px, the original) and the big-browser layout (1024px and up, added forty-ninth session) via `useBigBrowser()` and `.bb`-scoped CSS. Any session that touches a screen's UI must check and verify the change at both a phone viewport and a desktop viewport before calling it done; a change verified only at one size is not verified. New CSS for BB goes in `.bb`-scoped rules; mobile base rules are never edited for BB reasons. Screens share all state/logic across layouts; only arrangement differs.

## Gotchas (read before you re-debug something already debugged)

This section exists so a root cause, once found, never has to be found twice. Two kinds: real bugs whose *pattern* is likely to recur, and operational/tooling quirks specific to this environment.

### Bugs whose root cause is worth remembering

- **Reusing one field for two conceptually different purposes doesn't stay clear just because a UI picker filters differently in each context -- the field itself needs to actually be two fields once the two meanings genuinely diverge.** Multi-Coach Builder shipped by overloading the pre-existing `stations.team_staff_id` ("who leads this station live," a field that predates this feature) to *also* mean "who's delegated to design its content" -- narrowed to `can_build_practices`-eligible coaches in Builder's own picker, left unrestricted in the live Practice Setup reassignment picker (a different, lower-stakes concept). First attempt at fixing the resulting confusion added a `can_build_practices` re-check inside `update_station_content` itself, so a live-only reassignment couldn't silently also grant editing rights -- a real, necessary fix, but direct feedback ("how is it clear I want them to design that station rather than just be assigned to run what I tell them to run?") made clear the deeper problem wasn't fully solved: **there was still only one field, so a head coach had no way to look at a station and see two independent facts at once -- who's running it, and who's designing it.** Properly fixed by actually splitting the concept into two real columns: `stations.delegated_to` (new) is who's asked to plan the station's own content -- checked by `update_station_content`, what fires the assignment notice, what routes a coach into the restricted `MyStationBuilderScreen` -- while `stations.team_staff_id` reverted to exactly what it was before this feature ever touched it (live leader only, unrestricted picker, zero editing implication). Builder gained a new, separate "Plan This Station" control (next to Choose from Library, not folded into the existing Coach field) that only offers eligible coaches and shows a live Planned/Needs Planning status per delegated station, visible even collapsed and at the block level. **General lesson: a permission check added to patch over one field doing two jobs is a real fix for the security question, but not for the product question -- if a head coach can't tell which of two things they're setting by looking at the UI, the underlying data model still has one field where it needs two, and no amount of narrowing the picker or hardening the RPC actually resolves that.**
- **"Can this coach see/browse a drill" and "can this coach attach that exact drill to their own practice" are two separate RLS gates in this schema, and a feature that only updates the first one leaves the second one stuck in the past.** Forty-eighth session continued, direct report: a practice built from several Public Library drills plus one own-library drill showed everything correctly in Builder (drills visibly loaded with real duration/details), but "Run Now" silently saved only the one own-library drill -- Practice Setup then correctly rendered the real, truncated DB state, not a display bug. The browser console during the save had the real answer the whole time: `saveActivityTree insert activity: {code: 42501, ... "new row violates row-level security policy for table practice_activities"}`, once per public-library drill. Root cause: `practice_activities_insert_manage`'s `WITH CHECK` requires `can_link_drill_to_practice(library_activity_id)` -- a *different* function from `activity_library_select_access`/`can_access_activity` (the ones that let a coach browse/add a drill to their local Builder draft in the first place). The Public Library feature's own migration added a public-catalog branch to the SELECT-side functions when it shipped; `can_link_drill_to_practice` was never touched, because linking wasn't the thing being worked on that day. It got a peer-sharing branch added later (2026-08-01, `is_library_peer`), by copying the shape from `can_access_activity` -- which already had a public-catalog branch sitting right above it in the same file -- but the copy only picked up the branch actually being worked on, not the pre-existing gap next to it. Same story for `can_link_drill_to_template`/`_station`, not yet reported but certain to fail the identical way -- both predate Public Library entirely and never got a peer-sharing branch either. Fixed all three by adding a public-catalog branch (and, for the two template functions, the missing peer-sharing branch too) in `20260816010000_public_catalog_drill_linking_fix.sql`. **General lesson: whenever a drill/asset/whatever gets a new sharing mechanism, grep for every RLS function that gates "can this specific row be used *by* another row" (a `can_link_X_to_Y`-shaped function), not just the ones that gate "can this row be read" -- they're easy to miss because the read-side already working means the bug doesn't show up until someone actually tries to save, which can be a while after the read-side ships.** Separately confirmed peer-sharing linking itself already worked correctly once a coach's own "Share Drill Library" toggle was actually on -- the QA fixture team simply had it off, which is a correct denial, not a second bug; don't mistake an un-toggled sharing preference for a broken linking mechanism when re-testing this.
- **A client-side field split that distinguishes "real content" from "an auto-generated placeholder" doesn't survive a save if the two fields collapse into one DB column.** Forty-eighth session, building the Multi-Coach Builder completion indicator ("N of M stations filled in"): `addStation` seeds every new station's own `name` with a non-empty placeholder ("Station 2"), while `activityName` starts genuinely empty and only gets set once a coach types a real drill name or picks one from the library -- `StationConfig`'s own Name input reads `st.activityName||st.name`, so the placeholder is what actually displays and is editable from first render, easy to miss as "already has content." The first version of the completion indicator checked exactly that same fallback (`s.activityName||s.name`), which reads a totally untouched station as filled in. Worse, this isn't just a client-side display quirk: `saveActivityTree` persists `name: st.activityName||st.name||null` -- a single DB column -- so once *any* save happens, an untouched station's real DB row has `name="Station 2"` too, meaning the activityName/name distinction is gone for good after a reload, not just momentarily fooled before one. Fixed with a combined heuristic (`libraryId` set, `stationUpdatedAt` set, any real description/coaching-points/equipment, or a name that doesn't match the bare `Station \d+` placeholder pattern) that works whether the station was ever saved or not. **Caught live** (collapsed a block with one real station and one untouched default, read "2 of 2" instead of "1 of 2"), not in code review -- the bug looked correct reading the code in isolation, since `activityName||name` is exactly the right fallback for *display*, just wrong for *"has this been filled in."* **General lesson: before trusting a two-field split (a "real" field with an empty-until-set counterpart and a "display" field with a non-empty default) to answer a yes/no content question, check whether a save path ever writes the default into the same column the real field would occupy** -- the two fields can be genuinely different pre-save and functionally identical post-save.
- **An owner-scoped table's RLS policy can be correct for the library/template context it was written for and still be silently wrong for the live-practice context, if nobody ever added a branch for the second one.** Forty-seventh session, live co-testing as a head coach and an assistant on the exact same real practice: the assistant's own screen was missing the whole Equipment Needed section, every per-drill/per-station equipment pill, the practice's own location name, every Area label, and the transition screen's area summary (it read "Station 1 to Station 2" where the head coach saw real area names) -- all for the *same* practice, at the *same* moment. Root cause, found by testing the assistant's own real token directly against PostgREST rather than guessing from code: `assets_select_access` and `locations_select_access` were both, per their own original comments, "identical shape to activity_library" -- `can_access_owned` only (own, or org-owned-and-a-member), with **no branch at all** for "this row is referenced by a practice/station on a team I coach." That's correct for a *drill library* asset (an equipment item only matters there if it's shared some explicit way), but wrong for a *practice's own* equipment/location -- any co-coach who can already see and run the practice itself (`practice_activity_equipment`/`station_equipment`/`stations` all already grant that, correctly) still couldn't resolve the *names* of the things it referenced, because the referenced rows themselves stayed owner-only. Fixed by adding an `exists (... can_access_practice_activity/can_access_station/can_access_team ...)` branch to each (`20260813160000`, `20260813161000`) -- the general lesson: **any table with an owner-only RLS policy that's also referenced from inside a practice/station needs its own explicit "or it's used in a practice I can access" branch; being visible via the join table that points *at* it is not the same as the referenced row itself being visible**, and this class of gap won't show up testing solo as the practice's own owner, only co-testing as a second real coach. `activity_library` itself is not at risk of the same gap in practice, confirmed by design, not just luck -- `practice_activities`/`stations` copy every drill field (name, description, etc.) at add-time rather than referencing the library row live (see "Ownership patterns" above), so a co-coach never needs to resolve a library row's own RLS to see what's already been copied onto the practice. Worth a real audit of any *other* owner-scoped table joined to `practice_activities`/`stations`/`practice_activity_equipment`/`station_equipment` before assuming this pass caught every instance.
- **Drill/equipment tags "not persisting"**: `syncDrillTags`/`syncDrillEquipment` never checked `.error` on insert/delete, so a rejected write (e.g. a failed RLS check) looked identical to success — the modal closed and data silently reverted. Not a broken permission model; purely missing error surfacing. Fixed by propagating `{error}` through the whole chain and keeping the modal open with a visible error on failure.
- **"Today" rolling over early for western-hemisphere coaches**: `new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10)` converts to UTC before truncating, which rolls "today" to the next calendar day hours before local midnight west of Greenwich (surfaced by a Phoenix, UTC-7 coach). Fixed with `localDateStr()` (same local-getter construction Month view's grid already used correctly) swapped in everywhere the UTC pattern appeared.
- **Team creation broken for any coach with no last name**: the auto-head-coach trigger copied `profiles.last_name` (optional) straight into `team_staff.last_name` (`NOT NULL`), silently rolling back the whole `teams` insert with no UI error. Fixed with `coalesce(...,'')`.
- **Schedule duration field un-typeable**: `+e.target.value || 60` meant clearing the field to type a new number evaluated to `60` on every keystroke (`+"" || 60`), so a coach could never get past the leading digit. Fixed by allowing a transient empty-string state, defaulting only on blur/submit.
- **iOS auto-zoom on some inputs but not others**: the shared input font-size was 15px, one pixel under the 16px threshold where iOS Safari auto-zooms on focus. Bumped to 16px app-wide.
- **`org_staff` member list silently empty**: the table has two FKs to `profiles` (`user_id`, `invited_by`), so a bare `profiles(...)` PostgREST embed was ambiguous and errored — silently swallowed by the function's own `catch`, returning `[]`. Fixed with an explicit `profiles!org_staff_user_id_fkey(...)` hint.
- **A director seeing a stranger's pending org invite**: `org_invites_select`'s RLS deliberately lets a director see every pending invite for orgs they administer (needed for a sent-invites list) — but the client rendered all of those as if addressed to the viewer. Fixed by filtering client-side to the caller's own verified email, matching the same server-side-truth principle the accept/decline RPCs already use.
- **The under-planned pill's warning glyph**: `⚠` (U+26A0) renders with its own built-in emoji-yellow color in most browsers regardless of the CSS `color` applied to it — looked like a permanent yellow warning triangle no matter the pill's actual state. Removed; the red under-planned state deliberately has no icon now. Don't reintroduce one there without checking it isn't in the emoji-default-presentation set.
- **`useBlocker` + an unconditional per-render effect is a real infinite-loop risk**: PlayerProfile's leave-prompt work added `useBlocker` alongside a *pre-existing* effect that re-registered `subViewBack` on every render (no deps, safe on its own for years) — combined, they fed back into each other and threw React's "Maximum update depth exceeded" the instant the screen mounted. If you add `useBlocker` to a screen that already has any unconditional every-render effect touching shared/context state, register that effect once (a ref for "always see the latest closure" instead of "re-run every render") rather than assuming the existing pattern is still safe.
- **A modal fed a snapshot object at open time goes stale the moment its own action refetches data** (twenty-ninth session, `PermissionsModal`): `App.jsx` held `permissionsCoach` (state) as the *whole coach object*, captured once when a roster row was clicked. Toggling a permission called `refreshTeams()`, which correctly updated `data.teams` with a brand-new coach object reflecting the new value — but `permissionsCoach` itself was never reassigned, so the already-open modal kept rendering the old object's old value. The toggle only looked "stuck" until the coach closed and reopened it (which re-derived a fresh object from the click). Fixed by storing just the id and deriving the live object from `team.coaches` on every render instead. **Any `openX(fullObject)`-style modal/panel state that's meant to stay open across its own refetch should hold an id and re-derive, never the object itself** — check for this pattern (`useState` seeded from a list-item click, no re-derivation afterward) before shipping a new modal that mutates the thing it's displaying.
- **A write to `teams` with no matching `refreshTeams()` call leaves the top-level `data.teams` cache stale, and any control that re-derives its initial value from that cache will silently "forget" a successful save** (thirty-first session, `updateGoalsWindowWeeks`): the RPC/update itself worked every time, confirmed by querying the database directly — the bug was purely that nothing on the client ever told the app's central `data.teams` array to refetch, so a `useState` seeded from `team.goalsWindowWeeks` kept reading the old number on the next fresh mount (switching subtabs and back, or a hard reload), even though the true value had already changed server-side. The report itself looked fine immediately after saving (its own `refreshReport()` call was already correct) — only the control's own displayed value regressed, and only after navigating away, which made it easy to miss in a quick manual check. **Any direct `supabase.from('teams').update(...)` (or similar top-level entity write) needs its caller to also invoke that entity's own refresh function** (`refreshTeams()`, `refreshLibrary()`, etc.) **if any mounted component re-derives state from the cached copy of what it just wrote** — grep for other bare `.update()` calls against `teams`/`practices`/etc. before assuming a save "took" just because the RPC returned no error. **That audit was actually run, forty-first session** (see Session Log): the one real instance found was `GoalsScreen.jsx`'s History "Delete Practice" action, which called `archivePractice()` but only refreshed its own report/history queries (`onChanged`→`refreshAll`), never `refreshPlanning()` — fixed by adding that call, the same one-line fix as here.
- **A second, confirmed instance of the exact same pattern above, found live during the twenty-sixth session's Execution Scorecard verification — no `useBlocker` involved this time, so the bug isn't specific to that hook.** `SessionHistoryDetail`'s own `setSubViewBack({onBack})` effect depended on `onBack` directly, and `onBack` is a fresh closure every time `GoalsScreen` renders (`onBack={() => setOpenSessionId(null)}`) — so the effect re-ran on every render, called `setSubViewBack` with a *new* object every time (that state lives in `Layout.jsx`, an ancestor), which re-rendered Layout and everything it wraps including this route, which recreated `onBack` again, forever. This one had apparently been dormant/borderline for a while (the component predates this session) — the extra state-driven re-renders this session's new scorecard fetch introduced were seemingly what tipped it into an actual runaway (confirmed via a render counter: climbing unbounded within a second of opening any session, not merely "a bit more than before"). Fixed the same way as PlayerProfile's version: a ref holding the latest `onBack`, registering the effect once on `[setSubViewBack]` alone. **The general lesson, now confirmed twice**: any effect in this codebase that takes a freshly-created callback prop (an inline arrow function passed down fresh every render) as a dependency, and writes that callback into shared/ancestor state, is a latent infinite-loop risk regardless of whether `useBlocker` is anywhere nearby — audit for this shape specifically (`useEffect(() => { someAncestorSetter({callbackProp}) }, [..., callbackProp])`) before assuming an existing screen is safe to build on top of. **That audit was actually run, forty-first session** (see Session Log): of the four `setSubViewBack` call sites in the app, two had already gotten the ref fix (this one, and `App.jsx`'s own) but two hadn't — `HistoryViewer` (`CommandScreen.jsx`) and `PracticeDetail.jsx`, both live-risky specifically when reached via `ScheduleScreen.jsx` (which passes `setSubViewBack` alongside a fresh inline `onBack` arrow every render; reached via `HomeScreen.jsx`, which doesn't pass `setSubViewBack` at all, neither was ever at risk). Both fixed with the same ref pattern.
- **A report's "Planned" bucket means different things in different RPCs on purpose here — mixing them silently produces a real, confusing UI bug, not just a documentation nit.** `get_team_goal_report`'s Planned is forward-looking (currently *scheduled* practices); the new `get_team_goal_trends`' Planned is backward-looking (what was actually saved for each *completed* session, so it compares the same cohort as Actual). The first draft of `GoalTrendCard` mixed the two — showing the *report's* forward-looking Planned percentage as the card's headline number, right next to a chart plotting the *trends RPC's* backward-looking Planned line — which live-verified as a real "Planned 0%" summary sitting directly beside a chart visibly showing ~55% planned. Fixed by deriving the card's own rolling summary from an average of the same weekly data the chart already plots, never touching `get_team_goal_report` for anything Trends-specific. If a future report needs *both* concepts on screen at once, label them distinctly (e.g. "Currently Scheduled" vs. "Historical Average") rather than calling both just "Planned."
- **Navigating straight to `/builder/:practiceId` via a bare `navigate()` (instead of the existing `goToBuilder()` helper) silently loads the wrong practice — a real bug, found live while wiring Development Pulse's Builder deep link, not merely a hard-refresh quirk.** `BuilderScreen`'s local state (`existingId`, `teamId`, `acts`, `locId`, etc.) is seeded via `useState(editP ? ... : ...)`, where `editP = data.practices.find(p=>p.id===editPracticeId)` and `editPracticeId` lives in top-level context (`App.jsx`). `BuilderRoute` only sets that context from the URL's `:practiceId` param inside a `useEffect` — one render tick *after* mount — specifically because every existing entry point (`goToBuilder(practiceId)`) already sets it synchronously before calling `navigate()`, so this effect is meant only as a direct-link/refresh fallback. A caller that skips `goToBuilder` and calls `navigate("/builder/"+id)` directly lands on `BuilderScreen`'s first render with `editPracticeId` still `null` — `editP` is `null`, and every one of those `useState` initializers locks in "blank new practice" defaults that React then never re-derives, even once `editPracticeId` correctly updates a moment later (a `useState` initializer only runs once, on mount). The route and URL are both correct; only the rendered content is wrong (today's date, no scheduled duration, empty Run of Practice) — easy to miss unless you specifically check the practice's real saved fields against what's rendered. Fixed by extending `goToBuilder()` with an optional `navState` param (forwarded straight into `navigate()`'s own `{state}`) rather than adding a second, parallel way into Builder. **Any future deep link into Builder must go through `goToBuilder()`, never a bare `navigate("/builder/"+id)`.**
- **A document-level "click outside collapses this" listener that checks `.closest()`/`.contains()` can misfire the instant the clicked element removes itself from the DOM inside its own `onClick` — and this class of bug is easy to reproduce, not a one-off.** Direct feedback: in Builder's station-block editor, choosing "Dominant Hand" from the Group By dropdown, or a drill from a station's "Choose from Library" picker, kicked the coach out of the whole station block back to the collapsed Run of Practice view — they had to tap back in and lost their place. Root cause, confirmed by reading the code rather than guessing: both selections call a state setter (`setGroupByOpen(false)`, `setLibraryPickerIdx(null)`) that unmounts the clicked option element *before* a `document`-level `click` listener (added for "tapping outside the expanded row collapses it") runs later in the same event's bubble phase. `.closest()`/`.contains()` evaluate against the DOM *as it currently exists* — by the time the outside-click handler runs, the clicked element is already gone, so it looks identical to a click that was never inside the row at all, and the handler collapses it. Fixed by switching the outside-click check to `event.composedPath()`, which is captured at dispatch time (before any synchronous handler has a chance to mutate the tree) and lists every element the click actually passed through, immune to later DOM mutations. **Any future "click outside to close/collapse" listener in this codebase should default to `composedPath()` over `.closest()`/`.contains()`** unless there's a specific reason the current-DOM-state check is required — the failure mode (an option that unmounts itself on select) is common enough to hit again. A second fix in the same area, unconfirmed against the original live symptom but low-risk: `collapseAndScroll`'s `scrollIntoView` call, previously scheduled with one `requestAnimationFrame`, now nests a second one — a plausible race with the `ResizeObserver`-driven `runOfPracticeH` state (measuring the green backdrop's height) recalculating layout on the same frame a very tall station block collapses, which could land the scroll position mid-recalculation.
- **`.insert(rows).select()` does not guarantee the returned rows come back in the same order they were sent — zipping a response array against the original request array by index is a latent correctness risk, found (not yet confirmed to have caused a real observed bug, but a genuine one on inspection) while investigating a different, ultimately-false-alarm symptom in station-group saving** (thirty-second session continued, `saveSessionGroups`, `src/supabase.js`): the function inserts one row per player group, then zipped the returned `groupRows` against the original `groups` array positionally (`groupRows.forEach((gr,i) => groups[i]...)`) to attach each group's member-player-ids to the right just-created `group_id`. If PostgREST/Supabase ever returns `.select()` rows in a different order than the insert payload, this pairs a group's row with a *different* group's player list. Fixed to zip by the row's own `group_number` column instead (`groups[gr.group_number-1]`) — a stable key, not response order. **General lesson: any `.insert(rows).select()` call in this codebase whose result is consumed by array-index pairing against the original request array should be audited and switched to a real key-based join instead.**
- **`SortableActivityRow`'s per-row stacking context traps any popover inside it, and the fix generalizes beyond this one component.** Direct feedback: a drill's ellipsis dropdown menu in the Library's drag-sortable "My Drills" list was visually clipped by the *next* row's opaque background — including on the *last* row in the list, ruling out a simple "menu extends past the container" theory. Root cause: `SortableActivityRow` (`ActivityConfigs.jsx`, shared by Builder and the Library drill list) gives every row `position:relative` plus an explicit `zIndex` (at minimum `1`, even when not dragging/sticky) — that combination always creates a new CSS stacking context, and every row gets the *same* zIndex level, so z-index comparisons for something deep inside one row (a `.mini-menu` at `z-index:50`) only resolve *within that row's own stacking context* and can never escape to paint above a sibling row, which is a separate context painted after it in DOM order regardless of any z-index set inside the first one. No amount of raising the menu's own z-index fixes this — the trap is one level up. Fixed with a new `raised` prop on `SortableActivityRow` that bumps that specific row's own zIndex above its siblings while something inside it needs to render over what comes after it (`zIndex:10` when raised, vs. the normal `1`). Any future popover/dropdown living inside a `SortableActivityRow` (or any other component using this same "give every item a zIndex for legitimate reasons" pattern) needs the same treatment — check for exactly this shape before assuming a z-index bump on the popover itself will work.
- **A discarded/uncaptured Supabase `{error}` is this codebase's single most-repeated root cause, and it was still live in the single most central write path in the app.** Already confirmed four separate times before this (drill tags, Add Player, Save-as-Template, the consultation form) — forty-first session swept for more instances and found `savePracticeTree`'s own **update** branch (`src/supabase.js`, the path every Builder Save takes when editing an already-scheduled practice, the common case): the sibling **insert** branch two lines below it correctly checks `{error}` and returns early, but the update branch fired `.update(row)` with no captured return value at all, so a rejected update (RLS edge case, network blip) fell straight through into saving the activities/stations and returned a clean success — the coach would see "Saved" while the practice's own date/time/location/duration silently never changed. Fixed to match the insert branch. A second, related instance: `setPlannedAbsences` didn't even capture `{error}` from either of its two writes, and its caller (`AbsencePicker.save()`) never checked any of its three absence-write functions' return values before unconditionally closing the modal — meaning a rejected "who's out" save looked identical to a successful one, silently, while Builder's own station-assignment code trusts that data to keep an absent player off a station. Fixed both ends: the data-layer function now returns its errors, and the caller now surfaces them with a real error message and keeps the modal open, the same convention `ModalLayer.jsx` already established. **Any `await supabase.from(...).update/insert/upsert/delete(...)` whose result isn't captured into `{error}` and checked by its caller should be treated as a live footgun, not a style nit** — grep for bare `await supabase.from(` with no destructured return before assuming a write path is safe.
- **A reentrancy guard implemented with plain `useState` (`if (busy) return; setBusy(true)`) is unsafe under a fast double-tap, because `setState` doesn't take effect until the next render — a second call can pass the check before the first render commits.** This project already learned this once (`ModalLayer.jsx`'s `save()`, then ported to `SeriesWizard.jsx`'s `confirm()`) but the fix was never swept to other save/confirm actions with the same shape. Forty-first session found and fixed two more: `FuturePracticeGuardModal.jsx`'s `run()` (guards "Run This Plan as a New Practice," which inserts a brand-new practice row — a double-tap there could create two duplicate practices) and `AbsencePicker.jsx`'s `save()`. Both ported the same `useRef(false)` guard already proven in the other two spots. **Any Save/Confirm/Run action gated only by a `useState` boolean (not a paired `useRef`) is a candidate for this exact bug** — the `disabled={busy}` attribute on the button doesn't prevent it, since that disabling also only takes effect on the next render.
- **A "manager-only" screen's `canManage` flag controlling only copy, not visibility, is a real access-scoping bug, not just wording.** Forty-second session, `DevelopmentPulseCard`: `canManage` (from `canManageTeamInMode`) only ever swapped a few CTA labels between manage-oriented and view-oriented verbs -- the card itself rendered for any Coach-mode team regardless of whether the viewer actually head-coaches it, so an assistant who'd just accepted an invite saw a head-coach planning tool (goal-setting, Builder guidance) for a team they don't manage. Fixed by scoping the focus-team candidate pool itself to `isHeadCoach` teams (and ignoring `nextPractice` as a short-circuit when its team isn't one of them), not just relabeling once a team was already picked. **Before trusting a `canManage`/`canManageTeamInMode` prop to gate a manager-only surface, check whether it actually controls rendering or only copy -- a flag that's plumbed through but only read for wording is easy to mistake for a real access check.**
- **A destructive action's "who can trigger this" set should be checked against "who does the UI render the control for," not assumed identical -- a head coach could remove themselves from their own team's Coaches list.** Forty-second session, `RostersTab`'s roster ellipsis menu: the "Remove" option rendered for every coach row a manager (`canManage`) can see, with no check for whether the row being rendered *is the viewer's own*, and `archiveStaff` (a bare `.update()` on `team_staff`, not an RPC) had no server-side guard against it either -- unlike `leave_team`, which already refuses for team owners. Fixed both ends: the client hides "Remove" for a coach's own head-coach row, and a new trigger (`before_team_staff_archive_block_self_head_coach`, `20260809010000_prevent_head_coach_self_removal.sql`) blocks it at the table level too, scoped narrowly to `role='head_coach' AND user_id=auth.uid()` so `leave_team`'s legitimate self-archive path for assistants/helpers is untouched. **Any "remove/archive someone from a list" control rendered from a `.map()` over rows the viewer manages should be checked for whether it also renders on the viewer's own row, and whether removing themselves is actually a safe/intended action** -- it usually isn't, for whatever role holds the team's only real management rights.
- **Notification/permission data fetched once at login and never refetched in response to someone else's write is a recurring shape in this app, not a one-off.** Forty-second session: `data.teams`/`data.pendingTeamJoinNotices`/etc. (from `refreshTeams`/`refreshLibrary`) are only refetched on initial mount or after the *viewer's own* write -- a head coach already sitting on Home never learned an assistant had accepted an invite until they manually reloaded or fully quit and reopened the app, since nothing else ever triggers a refetch. A related but distinct bug on the same surface: the "Set Up Permissions" button on that same notice card navigated away without ever calling `acknowledgeTeamJoinNotice` (only the separate Dismiss button did), so acting on the card -- the expected path -- left the same prompt reappearing on every later Home visit; the team-departure card's "View Coaches" button had the identical gap. Fixed the acknowledge gap on both cards, and added a `visibilitychange`-triggered refetch plus a 60s foregrounded-tab poll (`App.jsx`, calling `refreshTeams`/`refreshLibrary`) as a general backstop, cheaper than standing up a realtime channel for every table this class of staleness could touch. **Any Home notification/notice card with more than one action button should be checked that *every* button which counts as "handling" the notice actually acknowledges it, not just the most obvious one** -- and any data that only updates in response to the viewer's own actions is a candidate for the same foreground/poll refresh if someone else's write can invalidate it.
- **A shared "advance the session" function that resets derived UI state unconditionally will clobber that state on every call, not just the ones that actually invalidate it.** Forty-fourth session, `transitionTo` (`CommandScreen.jsx`, the single function behind every advance/rotate/transition-state change in a live session): it called `setFocusSt(null)` on every invocation, correct for a real move to a different `current_practice_activity_id` (the coach's drilled-into station is genuinely gone), but also fired on a same-block *rotation*, where station identity stays at a fixed array index and only `.assignments` remaps -- so a station leader who'd tapped into their own station's detail view got bounced back to the all-stations overview on every single rotation, not just real activity changes. Fixed by gating the reset on `'current_practice_activity_id' in patch` instead of running it unconditionally. **Before adding a state reset to a shared multi-purpose transition/advance function, check whether every caller of that function actually invalidates the state being reset, or only some of them do** -- a reset that's correct for one call site and wrong for a sibling call site through the same shared function is easy to miss since it "mostly" looks right in casual testing.
- **A "what's live right now, anywhere" query needs its own staleness/archived filtering, because nothing in this app ever automatically expires a live session -- the only exit from `status='active'` is an explicit end/abort by a coach.** Forty-fourth session continued, `fetchActiveLiveSessions` (Home's top join bar, shipped forty-third session): the query was `eq('status','active')` and nothing else, correct for the intended case (a real practice someone forgot to check) but blind to the fact that `practice_live_sessions` rows can be arbitrarily old -- a month-old dev-test session (team archived weeks prior, never even started) surfaced as a real "live" practice with nothing in it, in production, the same day this banner shipped. Fixed narrowly (a 6-hour `created_at` cutoff plus an archived-team exclude), but this is a **shape**, not a one-off: any future query whose job is "find every X that's currently Y" over a table where Y is only ever set by an explicit user action (never automatically cleared) should ask whether an abandoned browser tab / crashed session / forgotten test artifact could leave a row stuck in that state forever, and filter for staleness explicitly rather than trusting the status column alone. See Known Gaps for the larger, still-open version of this problem (no auto-abandon mechanism exists for the underlying row itself, only for this one banner's visibility).
- **A maintenance-sweep function triggered by a polling client (not a real scheduled job) must be safe under concurrent invocation, and chained data-modifying CTEs are not, by default.** Forty-fourth session continued again, `abandon_stale_live_sessions()`: the first version found stale rows via one `SELECT` CTE, fed a `DELETE ... RETURNING` off that same snapshot, then an `UPDATE` using the delete's output -- all correct in isolation (verified via rolled-back `db query --linked` tests), but wrong the moment two calls overlap, which they do in practice: `fetchActiveLiveSessions` polls every 20s and a fresh page mount can fire it twice in quick succession (React's mount/cleanup/mount-again behavior in dev). Whichever call's `DELETE` lost the race found the row already gone (deleted by the other call, which hadn't necessarily committed yet under READ COMMITTED) and fell back to `ended_at = created_at`, silently overwriting the other call's correct value -- caught live (not in the isolated single-call tests) via a real seeded session ending up with `ended_at` identical to `created_at` when it should have been 20 minutes later. Fixed with the standard Postgres pattern for exactly this shape: `for update skip locked` in an explicit per-row loop, so a second concurrent call skips any row still locked by the first call's in-flight transaction instead of racing it. **Any function meant to be triggered opportunistically by a client poll (this project's stated alternative to standing up real scheduled-job infra) needs to assume it will sometimes run concurrently with itself, and a single-call correctness test proves nothing about that case** -- test the actual double-invocation, or default to `for update skip locked` for any sweep-and-mutate shape from the start.
- **A `position:sticky` element that's taller than the remaining viewport space below its own `top` offset permanently clips its own overflowing content -- scrolling further never reveals more of it, because a stuck element doesn't move at all once stuck; only whatever comes *after* it in the document scrolls.** Forty-fourth session continued a fourth time, Builder's last-drill-stays-pinned feature (see above): the sticky condition was keyed on `isLast` alone, with no check for whether the row was currently expanded. A collapsed row is always a short single line, harmless to pin -- but the SAME row, expanded (auto-expand-on-add means this is the *common* case for a freshly added station block, not an edge case), can easily be 1000+px tall once its block-level controls (rotate/static, steppers, generate-random) plus every station's own name/equipment/player pickers are all showing. Direct feedback: "I'm building a station block and I'm unable to scroll down far enough to see the bottom options" -- exactly this trap, since the block was both the last activity *and* expanded, so it pinned itself with most of its own height permanently below the visible viewport, unreachable no matter how far the coach scrolled. Fixed by gating stickiness on `isLast && !expanded` -- an expanded row is never sticky, period, regardless of position in the list. **Any `position:sticky` usage on a row/card whose height can vary a lot (an expandable/editable one especially) needs an explicit check that it's short enough to safely pin, not just "is this the one I want to keep visible"** -- being the right *semantic* candidate for stickiness doesn't make an element safe to actually stick if it can grow arbitrarily tall while in that state.
- **The "4-gradient diagonal" CSS recipe makes a diamond/checkerboard tile, not a zigzag -- they're easy to conflate from memory, but only one of them reads as a torn/sawtooth edge.** Forty-fourth session continued a third time, Builder's new "torn edge" scroll indicator: the first pass used four 45°-stepped `linear-gradient`s (135/225/315/45deg) tiled together, which is the correct recipe for a diamond/polka-dot pattern -- at the narrow single-column width used here, it rendered as a row of small crosses/plus-marks, not a zigzag, caught immediately from a live screenshot. Replaced with an explicit `clip-path: polygon(...)` triangle-wave -- a real repeating comb shape, solid-colored by default (no clip-path, straight edge) and only clipped into teeth in the "on" state -- which is unambiguous to reason about geometrically (list the actual x/y vertices) in a way gradient-tile math isn't from memory alone. **When a CSS pattern needs to read as a specific recognizable shape (zigzag, chevron, wave) rather than just "textured," prefer `clip-path`/an explicit polygon over recalled gradient recipes** -- multiple genuinely different classic tricks (diamond tiling, chevron/zigzag, diagonal stripes) all involve angled `linear-gradient`s and are easy to misremember as each other; a polygon's vertices are directly verifiable by reasoning through them, a tiled gradient's visual result generally isn't without actually rendering it. **This still wasn't the end of it, though** -- two more rounds of direct feedback followed (scope: wanted one full-width divider, not two small edge caps; then shape: wanted an actual EKG heartbeat pulse-then-flatline, not a repeating mountain-range zigzag, referencing a screenshot). The final version dropped `clip-path` entirely for a plain SVG `<polyline>` -- once the target shape is a genuine *stroked line* (not a two-tone fill/cut boundary), an SVG path is the more direct tool than trying to fake a line via a filled polygon's boundary. **The broader lesson: when a request names a specific, referenceable real-world shape (a heartbeat monitor, a saw blade, a stamp perforation), treat that reference as the spec, not just inspiration for "some kind of jagged pattern"** -- three iterations here each partially matched ("jagged," yes; "zigzag," yes; "spans the full width," yes) while still missing the one specific shape actually being asked for, and each mismatch only became obvious once shown a live screenshot next to what was wanted.
