- **A "linked but unowned" fallback in a picker helper needs its own type filter, or it leaks across unrelated pickers on the same drill.** Forty-fourth session, the new `equipmentPickerAssets` helper (`ActivityConfigs.jsx`): its first version searched the *full* unfiltered asset list by id for any selected-but-not-in-pool item (the "drill references equipment the coach doesn't own" case), with nothing constraining the search to the calling picker's own asset type -- so a team-type placeholder asset showed up in the Player Gear picker too, and vice versa, caught live before shipping (Edit Drill modal showed the same unowned item under both sections at once). Fixed by adding an explicit `isType` predicate parameter, applied at all 6 call sites (drill-level team/player pickers, the per-station team/player pickers, and the drill editor modal's team/player pickers). **Any shared picker helper that falls back to searching a broader/unfiltered source list for an edge case should apply the same type/scope filter the caller's normal pool already had -- an unfiltered fallback silently reintroduces cross-category leakage the normal path was already preventing.**
- **A live view that trusts a single Realtime `postgres_changes` subscription as its only source of truth will silently desync on real-world flaky wifi, because Realtime has no catch-up/replay -- an update that happens while a client's websocket is disconnected is simply never delivered once it reconnects.** Forty-fifth session, `CommandScreen`'s `session` state (the coach's live-session view): the write path (`writeSession`/`takeControl`) already had real offline handling -- optimistic-concurrency version checks, retry-with-backoff, a genuine "network down" vs. "someone else moved the session" distinction -- but the read/sync path had none of that. One dropped realtime event and a client's local `session` object is stuck on stale data (wrong timer, wrong drill, wrong station rotation) until something else happens to deliver a payload it doesn't miss; the controller's own screen always looked right only because their writes update local state directly, not because sync was actually working. This is also why "Take Control" from stale state looked like it did nothing on the first tap -- the version-conflict path silently resyncs without taking control, so the tap "worked" invisibly and a second tap was needed to actually claim it. **Any screen whose state is meant to be shared live across multiple real devices needs an explicit reconciliation backstop independent of the realtime channel itself -- a forced refetch on every channel reconnect (not just the first `SUBSCRIBED`) plus a low-frequency background poll -- because "the websocket looks connected" and "no updates were missed while it wasn't" are two different claims, and a flaky network guarantees the gap between them gets exercised.** See Live sessions in the Feature Inventory for the actual fix.
- **An unhandled promise rejection inside a `useEffect`'s async IIFE doesn't crash anything visibly -- it just silently abandons whatever that effect was supposed to set, with nothing left to retry it, which reads to the user as a feature going blank for no reason.** Forty-fifth session, `fetchLatestGroups` (`src/supabase.js`) fed two `CommandScreen` effects (station-block groupings and per-station sub-groupings) that together drive a station block's live "Up Next"/rotation view -- the reported "Up Next in stations went blank" bug. The function only handled a clean Postgrest `{error}` result, not a genuine network-level throw, and neither calling effect had a try/catch around its own IIFE -- a hard throw on bad venue wifi meant the effect's promise chain just stopped, leaving `liveGroups`/`stationSubGroups` at `null`/`{}` for the rest of that mount, silently, with no error shown and no retry ever attempted. Fixed at both layers: the fetch function itself now catches and returns `null` like its clean-error path already did, and both effects got a small bounded (3-attempt, short-backoff) retry around their own body. **Any `useEffect` async IIFE that sets state other code depends on rendering needs its own try/catch with a retry or a visible failure state -- letting a network throw propagate unhandled inside an effect is functionally the same as "this feature intermittently stops working," just with no error anywhere to explain why.**
- **Correction to the entry above on `writeSession`/`takeControl` "already had real offline handling": the version check being correct in isolation didn't mean concurrent calls to it were safe.** Forty-fifth session continued, same day: the ±1 min timer buttons felt "delayed and jolty" on real hardware, root-caused to `writeSession` reading `session.version` from whichever render happened to be current at call time, not at actual-request time -- two rapid writes from the *same* client (±1 tapped twice quickly, a nudge right after a pause) could both read the same version, race each other to the server, and have the loser rejected as a stale-version conflict, silently discarding its own optimistic update via the existing (correct, for a *genuine* external conflict) resync path. Visibly: the timer jumps on the tap, then snaps back a beat later. This is a different failure mode from an external coach's write conflicting -- it's the same client's own rapid-fire actions racing themselves. Fixed by queuing every write through a chained promise (`writeQueueRef`) so a queued write only executes once every write ahead of it in the same client has resolved, reading a ref kept synchronously authoritative (not just mirrored via a `useEffect`, which lags a render cycle) instead of a closure captured at call time. **Any UI control a user might reasonably tap more than once in quick succession, backed by an optimistic-concurrency write, needs its writes serialized client-side -- a version check alone only proves any *single* write is safe against a conflict, not that a burst of the user's own writes won't conflict with each other.**
- **A threshold check that requires landing inside a narrow time window on a periodic tick will silently miss the window entirely if the tick itself can be delayed or skipped, which browsers do routinely to backgrounded tabs.** Forty-fifth session continued, the "two minutes remaining" audio cue: it fired only when `rem2` (seconds remaining) was inside `[118,122]`, evaluated inside a `useEffect` keyed off `elapsed`, which itself only advances while a `setInterval` ticks -- and mobile browsers throttle or fully suspend that interval whenever the tab is briefly backgrounded (an app switch, a notification), which real coaching on a real field guarantees happens sometimes. A throttled tick can jump `elapsed` clean over a 4-second window in a single step, silently skipping the cue for the rest of that phase with zero trace anything went wrong -- part of the reported "audio wasn't consistent." The time's-up buzzer effect right next to it never had this problem, because it already used an at-or-past check (`elapsed>=phaseSecs`) rather than a window; the warning cue was fixed to match (`rem2<=120&&rem2>0`). **Any effect meant to fire "at" a moment in time, driven by a value that updates on a timer rather than a direct event, should check "have we reached or passed this point" (with a guard against re-firing), not "are we currently sitting exactly at this point" -- a window can be jumped over, a threshold can't be un-crossed.**

### Operational gotchas worth not re-learning
- `supabase config push` (verified against the actual Supabase CLI Go source, not guessed) unconditionally overwrites only `site_url`/`additional_redirect_urls`; everything else (SMTP, email templates, MFA, rate limits) is nil-gated and untouched unless locally set in `config.toml` — check those two values are still correct production values before ever running this, not stale localhost/dev placeholders. Piping `"n"` to its interactive per-service confirmation prompts did **not** reliably decline in past testing — treat any piped-input dry run against this CLI with suspicion. Its per-service `up_to_date` output on a completed push is a **success** status, not a "nothing changed" signal — don't read it as a no-op.
- `service_role` bypasses RLS but not base-table GRANTs (see RLS conventions above) — this has caused silent empty results from more than one new Edge Function.
- No automated test suite exists anywhere in this project, by design — there's no local Supabase environment in this setup, so everything is verified empirically in a live browser against production, using disposable test accounts, before being called done.
- Edge Functions are **not** auto-deployed by pushing to `main` (only the Vercel frontend is) — `npx supabase functions deploy <name>` is required any time an edge function or a shared file it bundles (`_shared/*.ts`) changes. Every function that imports a changed shared file needs its own redeploy; they each bundle their own copy at deploy time, not a live shared reference. **`supabase functions deploy <name>` with no flags defaults to `verify_jwt:true`** (forty-fourth session, `notify-consultation-request`) — silently rejects a `pg_net` trigger's unauthenticated call with no useful error client-side. Any function meant to be called by `pg_net`/a webhook rather than a signed-in user needs `--no-verify-jwt` explicitly; check `npx supabase functions list`'s own output against this project's other pg_net-triggered functions (`verify_jwt:false`) if a new one mysteriously never fires.
- `read_console_messages` returned an identical stale error after a real fix landed, across a full dev-server restart and several genuinely fresh page loads (confirmed via `script[type=module]` src querystrings) — don't treat it as authoritative when re-verifying a fix in a long-running tab/session. Cross-check with an independently in-page-patched `console.error` counter (or a fresh screenshot/interaction check) before concluding a fix didn't work. **Reconfirmed useful this session** for a different reason too: the counter technique is also how a real infinite-render-loop bug (see Gotchas above) was confirmed as *actually looping* rather than stale/buffered output — a raw error count climbing across two back-to-back reads, immune to any buffering question, is a stronger signal than either a screenshot or the console-messages tool alone. **Reconfirmed a third time, thirty-second session continued, and this occurrence is worth its own note because it nearly triggered a wasted "fix"**: after a live station-player-move, `saveSessionGroups` appeared to be throwing (`read_console_messages` showed two stale `saveSessionGroups: [object Object]` entries) *and* a direct DB query showed zero `session_groups` rows — looking like a genuine, reproducible bug in the new Practice Setup station-reassignment code. The DB symptom turned out to be self-inflicted: manual `curl` inserts made *during the investigation itself* (probing whether RLS/schema allowed the write at all, done via the real REST API with the test account's own JWT — both single-row and batch inserts succeeded, ruling out RLS) had polluted `fetchLatestGroups`' "resolve latest by matching `created_at`" logic for that exact session+activity key, since the curl rows' timestamps didn't line up with the app's own subsequent writes. After deleting the contaminating rows and re-testing clean (fresh seed, fresh page load, an in-page `console.error` patch), the write worked correctly end-to-end and the two console errors never recurred — they were leftover buffered output from a much earlier interaction in the same long-lived browser tab, consistent with the two prior entries above. **Compounding lesson beyond "don't trust `read_console_messages`": manually inserting rows into an app table *while debugging that same table's "latest row" resolution logic* can itself corrupt the very state you're trying to observe** — prefer fully scripted seed/cleanup (a fresh practice, a fresh page load) over ad hoc `curl`/SQL probes against a table whose read path depends on relative row ordering or timestamps, or clean up probe rows immediately and re-test from a known-clean state before drawing any conclusion.
- **`supabase db diff --linked` and `db push --dry-run` don't do what their names suggest in this environment.** `db diff`/`db push --dry-run` both require a local Docker shadow database to actually diff or dry-run SQL against (`Cannot connect to the Docker daemon`) — with no Docker in this setup (same "no local Supabase environment" constraint as everywhere else in this file), `db push --dry-run` silently degrades to only checking the CLI's own applied-migrations bookkeeping table, never actually executing a single line of the pending SQL. The real dry-run mechanism this project's own Working Conventions ask for (`BEGIN;...ROLLBACK;`) needs a different command: `supabase db query --linked --file <path>`, which runs arbitrary SQL directly against the linked production database via the Management API — wrap a migration file's contents in `BEGIN;`/`ROLLBACK;` in a scratch file and pass that. This also validates runtime correctness, not just syntax: adding `set local role authenticated; select set_config('request.jwt.claim.sub','<a real user's uuid>',true);` before calling a `security definer` RPC lets you exercise its real `can_access_team`/`can_manage_team` authorization checks and see real output against real data, still safely rolled back. Twenty-sixth session used this to validate all five new RPCs against the real coach's own production data before ever running `db push` for real.
- **Two browser tabs of the same tool/profile share one origin's `localStorage` -- writing a second disposable account's Supabase session token into "a second tab" silently clobbers the first tab's session too, since Supabase's client syncs auth state across tabs via the `storage` event.** Forty-second session, testing a head-coach/assistant-coach pair live: setting the assistant's `sb-<ref>-auth-token` in what was meant to be an isolated second tab immediately signed the *first* (head-coach) tab in as the assistant instead, surfacing as a confusing "my saved name reverted to blank" symptom before the actual cause (shared storage, not a real data bug) was traced. **Don't try to run two authenticated disposable-account sessions in two tabs of the same Browser-pane/profile at once.** Two options that actually work: a genuinely separate browser/profile (e.g. a different browser tool entirely) for the second identity, or -- what this session used successfully -- keep everything in one tab and perform the "other party"'s actions via an isolated `fetch()` call with an explicit `Authorization: Bearer <their token>` header instead of a real signed-in tab, which never touches `localStorage` or the page's own Supabase client at all. The latter also works for simulating "someone else's write happened while my tab was already open," which is otherwise hard to reproduce with only one real browser session available.

---



## Persistent design themes (longer-running decisions, not single sessions)

These track *why* something is shaped the way it is, across many sessions — read the numbered sub-entries in order where present, since the design evolved.

### Auth
- Email OTP chosen over magic links specifically because magic links broke for the installed-homescreen-PWA sign-in case. `admin.inviteUserByEmail` is avoided for staff invites for the same reason (it's a magic link) — a custom `pg_net` + Resend Edge Function handles that notification instead.


### Skill tags & taxonomy
- Skill tags are seeded as **personal** `scope='coach'` copies per coach rather than true `scope='global'` rows: global rows are only writable by `service_role`/`is_org_admin` under existing RLS, so one coach archiving a shared global tag would silently remove it for every other coach. Per-coach seeding sidesteps that with zero RLS changes, reusing the machinery that already existed for a coach's own custom tags. `ensureDefaultSkillTags` re-runs (idempotently) on every sign-in so a coach picks up new tags from later migrations, not just what existed at signup.
- `skill_categories` has no app-level INSERT policy anywhere, by design — Jax seeds/curates these directly. This is why 4 of the app's 12 sports (Softball, Hockey, Tennis, Swimming) still have zero taxonomy: nobody has seeded them yet, not a technical blocker.
- A unified taxonomy and public-catalog drill content were drafted for a 6-sport initial pass (JSON-per-sport deliverable, so 150+ drills could be imported mechanically rather than hand-transcribed) with category names deliberately reused across sports on purpose (enables cross-sport reporting). A system publisher profile (e.g. `content@runofpractice.com`) was the chosen design for content attribution over relaxing ownership constraints — real `profiles` row means the existing `activity_has_owner` constraint needs no changes, and gives real "Published by Run of Practice" attribution for free. **This plan is what became the shipped Public Library feature** (see Library section above) for Baseball, Basketball, Football, Soccer, Lacrosse, and Volleyball — the original planning doc undersold this by never being updated to say so. Softball/Hockey/Tennis/Swimming were never part of this pass and still have no categories, tags, or public-library content.


### Sharing & org model (schema evolved twice — read in order)
1. **Original design (2026-07-06)**: a single `shared_with_organization_id` column on `activity_library`/`templates` — private by default, share with exactly one org, org-owned "Org Library" kept as a separate concept from coach-owned-shared-into-org "From Our Coaches." Public visibility explicitly deferred entirely (became the Public Library work above). The "copy, don't reference" rule was set here specifically to avoid a shared thing referencing something its viewers can't see (this bug class was hit five times during the original schema build) — copying a shared drill resolves equipment by name into the recipient's own pool rather than pointing at the sharer's rows.
2. **First org-model implementation (2026-07-21)**: `organization_members` (owner/admin/coach/viewer, zero real rows ever) was replaced wholesale by `org_staff` (single `director` role, extensible without a migration later). New RPCs (`org_create_team`, `org_assign_team_staff`, `org_assign_player`) were added as explicit auditable entry points, even though the underlying RLS already permitted an org-admin's direct write — deliberately not narrowed, since narrowing wasn't asked for. The single-org `shared_with_organization_id` column was recognized as insufficient the same day (a coach can be director of one org while coaching a team in a different org — a single column can't hold "share with more than one"), so it was replaced by the `activity_library_org_shares` join table before this design ever shipped as originally spec'd. `org_invites` got a real accept/decline consent flow (never the informal `team_staff` auto-link pattern) specifically because joining an org is a bigger commitment than being added to a team roster.
3. **Rework into Coach/Org mode (later session)**: the standalone Org Home page (`/org/:orgId`) was retired and folded directly into `HomeScreen.jsx` behind a persisted Coach/Org toggle — this is why `fetchPendingOrgInvites`/`orgAssignTeamStaff`/`orgAssignPlayer`/`promoteDrillToOrgLibrary` ended up as dead exports (superseded by different call patterns) cleaned up in the thirteenth session above. Team/asset/location/skill-tag creation inside Org mode reuses the same coach-or-org ownership pattern the rest of the app already had (`createOrgLocation`/`createOrgAsset`/`createOrgSkillTag` — no new RPCs needed, existing RLS already permitted a director's direct insert with `organization_id` set).


### Navigation (restructured multiple times — read in order)
1. Home originally had a pill/chip row for picking a team — removed entirely (not reskinned) once direct user feedback said it read as a filter control (tap to toggle in place) rather than navigation (tap to leave the page). Replaced by a dedicated global **Teams** tab, reachable from anywhere including Library.
2. The team workspace went from a 4-tab shape (Schedule/Plan/Goals/Team) to a 3-tab shape (Schedule/Plan/Team, Goals folded into Plan as a sub-toggle) — Plan's original scope had never actually been built past a placeholder, so nothing real was lost merging it in. Team Equipment/Player Gear moved out of global Settings into the Team tab and became sport-aware (auto-stamps the current team's sport, filters lists to `sport === team.sport || 'General'`) once showing all-sport gear while coaching one team's context was flagged as "weird." Skill Tags and Locations stayed in global Settings deliberately — they're coach-wide vocabulary/resources with no team ownership in the schema, so editing them from inside one team's context risked silently rippling to every other team.
3. Later flattened further: the nested Schedule/Plan/Team (with Plan hiding a Build/Goals toggle, Team hiding a Roster/Equipment toggle) became 5 direct top-row routes so nothing was two taps deep — then reduced to the current 4 (Schedule/Roster/Equipment/Goals & Insights) once Build's tab-bar entry was dropped as redundant with Home/Schedule's own "+ Practice" entry points (its route/component lived on as dead code until the thirteenth session).
4. The original `App.jsx` `view`-string state machine with manual `window.location.pathname` regex checks was replaced by real `react-router-dom` v6 routes; the `guardedSetView`/`priorView` unsaved-changes mechanism was replaced by `useBlocker` + `beforeunload` (the old mechanism never protected against a hard refresh or tab close either).
5. The old single "Manage" screen mixed coaching content and one-time setup in one junk drawer. Split: `SettingsScreen.jsx` (Account, Locations, Equipment & Gear, Skill Tags) for setup; Library's My Library/Explore structure for content; `ManageScreen` now purely serves the team workspace's Team tab. A critical catch made during planning, not after: the old Manage "My Teams" list held the app's only reachable "+ Team" button — it was rehomed onto the new Teams tab's header before the old list was deleted, not after.


### Landing page & legal
- No real product screenshots on the landing page, and no stylized mockups either — visuals are built from the real app's own CSS classes with realistic sample data, so they stay pixel-consistent with the actual UI as it changes, with one-line prop slots left for real screenshots later. Copy is deliberately sport/level-agnostic ("coaches"/"players", not "youth"/"kids"/"parents"), per an explicit positioning decision.
- An inline landing-page feedback form was removed after the user felt the copy read as AI-generated (em dashes, listy cadence) — em dashes and choppy list prose were removed project-wide on that page at the same time. The backing `submit_public_feedback` RPC/table were deliberately kept (see Library section) in case a future non-authenticated contact path wants them.


### Live sessions & timing
- The "leave a live session without ending it" feature was judged safe specifically because the live-run timer/stage was already fully re-derived from `session.current_phase_started_at` on mount (built earlier for multi-coach handoff/reconnect) — unmount/remount naturally resumes correctly, no separate persistence needed.
- Real audio-cue files via `<audio>` elements plus a silent background loop were chosen over an earlier, abandoned approach (a raw `AudioContext` oscillator tone) because Safari blocks Web Audio output but has a real background-audio exemption for actual media elements. The new tone was added strictly additively alongside the existing, working `speechSynthesis` cues first, specifically so a tone failing on some device wouldn't break the fallback — the redundant spoken "Next up!" cue was only removed later, after confirming the tone worked. `HelperView` (parent-facing) was deliberately not given the same treatment — the original complaint was specifically about the coach's own phone locking mid-session.
- `session_activity_log` has no stored/computed duration column (duration = `ended_at - started_at`, null if never explicitly ended) and no backfill/cleanup mechanism for a dangling open row from an abandoned session — flagged as needing a product decision (exclude? clamp to session end? clamp to now?) with no existing convention to follow yet.


### Goals & Insights architecture (twenty-sixth session)
- Every category-attribution calculation across Trends, Next Practice Guidance, Builder Goal Guidance, the Execution Scorecard, and Drill Insights is built on the same four small SQL functions and one composite type (`activity_minute_row`), extracted once from `get_team_goal_report`'s own logic rather than copied five more times — this project already paid for that mistake once (the `get_team_goal_report`/`set_team_goals` stale-column incident, seventeenth session) and the whole point of extracting shared functions instead of shared *documentation* is that a fix to the real logic only has to happen in one place. The functions are deliberately `security definer` with `revoke all from public` (not `grant ... to authenticated`) — they take a bare id with no authorization check of their own, trusting the outer RPC to have already verified access, which only works because PostgREST can't reach them directly once PUBLIC execute is revoked. Don't add a new one of these without the same revoke, or it becomes a direct, unauthenticated-relative-to-content data leak.
- Two genuinely different "Planned" concepts coexist on purpose (`get_team_goal_report`'s forward-looking vs. `get_team_goal_trends`'s backward-looking, completed-session-cohort version) — see the Gotcha above for the real bug this caused once already when a component quietly assumed they were interchangeable. Any future report that needs its own notion of "planned" should ask which cohort it actually wants before reusing either RPC's number by convention.
- Builder's projection math (`categoryMinutesForPracticeActivities`/`calculateProjectedGoalImpact`) is intentionally pure client-side JS, not a server round trip per keystroke — the baseline (rolling history) is fetched once per team selection and cached; every recalculation as the draft changes is a local `useMemo`. This is the same "don't send the unsaved draft to a new RPC on every change" instruction the original spec called for, and mirrors how Builder's own local `acts` state has always worked (nothing round-trips to the server until Save).
- The Execution Scorecard and Drill Insights share one on-plan tolerance constant (`ON_PLAN_TOLERANCE_SECONDS = 60`, `classifyDurationVariance`) specifically so "extended"/"shortened"/"on plan" means the same thing regardless of which screen a coach is looking at — a practice-level scorecard and a drill-level insights view giving different verdicts on the same underlying timing data would undermine trust in both.


## Session Log (chronological history — append new sessions here)

Every session's work gets one dated, numbered entry below, **newest first**. When you finish a session:

1. Write a new entry using the same voice as the ones below: what was asked, what was actually built (call out anything that turned out to already exist, or that you deliberately didn't build), any real bug found along the way (not just requested changes), and how it was verified.
2. Insert it at the **top** of this section (immediately below this instructions block), not the bottom — this list reads newest-first.
3. Update "Last updated" and the Status line at the top of this file to reflect the new latest session.
4. If the session changed a known gap, a working convention, added a real gotcha, or changed the feature inventory, edit that section directly — don't rely on someone reading the whole log to find out the app now works differently.

Sessions are grouped by theme rather than strictly re-sorted when a later session revisits an earlier decision — check the inline dates in each entry if exact ordering matters. Numbering is sequential per work session (a same-day follow-up is "Nth session continued," not a new number).

### Forty-ninth session (2026-08-16): Big Browser layout pass, Foundation and Builder

Jax handed off a spec (`HANDOFF-big-browser-coach-screens.md`) for adding a big-browser (BB, >=1024px) layout to every logged-in coach screen while leaving mobile pixel-identical -- single codebase, adaptive layout shells, no forked logic, screen-by-screen build sequence with verification at each step. Read this file's own Gotchas/Known Gaps/Working Conventions first, then had two Explore agents ground the plan in the actual code (Builder's sticky/observer machinery, and the other screens' render structure) before writing anything, since the handoff's own description of the code was necessarily written without re-reading it line by line. Confirmed nothing like `useBigBrowser`/`matchMedia`/a second stylesheet existed anywhere -- this was greenfield infrastructure, not a variant of something already there.

Shipped this session, per the user's own chosen pacing (stop after Foundation + Builder for review before the remaining 8 screens): the `useBigBrowser()` hook (`src/constants.js`, `matchMedia('(min-width:1024px)')` with a change listener); the `.bb` class wired onto `.app` in `src/Layout.jsx`; a `.bb`-scoped CSS block appended after the existing rules in `src/App.jsx` (no existing selector edited -- confirmed by construction, not just care, since `.bb` literally cannot apply below the breakpoint); a left sidebar rail replacing the bottom tab bar at BB, reusing `GLOBAL_TABS`'/`teamWorkspaceTabs`' exact same active-state logic, plus a BB-only Settings item (`Ic.Gear`, a new icon reusing the same path as Home's existing gear button) since the handoff asked for the gear to become "a bottom-of-rail item"; centered-dialog `.bb`-scoped CSS for `.movly`/`.modal` bottom sheets; and `src/components/BBShells.jsx` (`TwoPane`, `CenteredPage`, layout-only, no logic).

Then Builder (`BuilderScreen`, `src/App.jsx`) itself: at BB, the existing JSX for Practice Details/Builder Goal Guidance/the Run of Practice section became `leftPaneContent`, and Practice Components/My Drill Library became `rightPaneContent`, rendered via `<TwoPane left right/>` with each pane independently `overflow-y:auto`; at mobile, the exact same two content variables render in the same single column as before, unchanged. The Save/Run Now sticky bar was hoisted into its own `stickyBarContent` variable so it renders at the top of the left pane at BB (per the handoff's own recommendation) or in its original position at mobile -- same ref, same handlers either way. `MyStationBuilderScreen` got the lighter version (own-station editor left, read-only sibling summary cards right via the same `TwoPane`), since it has none of Builder's sticky/observer machinery.

The real risk in this pass was whether Builder's sticky Save/Run Now bar, sticky green header, EKG "torn edge" scroll indicator, and last-drill pinning -- all built years before BB existed, implicitly assuming `.screen` was the only scroll container in the app -- would survive being nested inside a brand-new `overflow-y:auto` pane. They did, with zero code changes: the `ResizeObserver`s measure via `getBoundingClientRect()`/`offsetHeight` (pure layout math, agnostic to which ancestor scrolls), and the one `IntersectionObserver` already used `root:null`, which the browser resolves against the nearest actual clipping ancestor regardless of whether that's `.screen` or a new nested pane -- confirmed live, not just reasoned about, by scrolling the left pane with a real multi-activity plan and watching the sticky bar/header/EKG all behave correctly while the right pane stayed completely static.

Verified live end-to-end with the persistent QA head-coach account (`ropqa-head`, "QA Persistent Wolves" team) at 1440x900, exactly 1024px (and just below it, at 1023px, to confirm the boundary falls back to mobile), and 390x844 mobile: scheduled a practice, opened it in the two-pane Builder, added a drill from the right pane (including the equipment-mismatch dialog for the QA fixture drill's deliberately-unowned equipment, which itself rendered as a correctly-centered BB dialog), added Practice Components, collapsed the last row to trigger sticky pinning, confirmed independent per-pane scrolling directly via `scrollHeight`/`clientHeight` DOM introspection (left pane scrollable, right pane not, exactly matching their actual content), saved, reloaded, and confirmed all 5 activities and the equipment flag persisted correctly. Resized a live window with an unsaved draft across the breakpoint in both directions (BB to mobile and back) and confirmed zero state loss -- `BuilderScreen`'s component instance never remounts across the branch, only which JSX wraps `leftPaneContent`/`rightPaneContent` changes, exactly as planned. `npm run build` and the full 53-test `vitest` suite both pass throughout. One tooling note, not an app bug: `read_console_messages` returned stale buffered `[hmr]`/500 errors from mid-edit dev-server reloads that persisted across reloads and even a full dev-server restart in the same long-lived browser tab -- matches this file's own documented `read_console_messages` staleness gotcha; a fresh tab confirmed zero real errors.

Not verified this session, flagged rather than glossed over: real mouse-drag reordering via dnd-kit inside the new left-pane scroll container (the code path is unchanged and dnd-kit's own default auto-scroll re-discovers scrollable ancestors per drag, but no live drag gesture was actually performed), and `MyStationBuilderScreen`'s BB rendering with a real delegated-coach account (lower risk given it has none of Builder's sticky/observer complexity, but only confirmed via code reading and build/tests here). See Known Gaps for these plus the remaining 8 screens still queued.

### Forty-ninth session continued (2026-08-16): Big Browser layout pass, Home's two-column dashboard

Continuing the BB pass per the handoff's own build sequence, next up was Home (`HomeScreen.jsx`). Its render was one long single column mixing three different kinds of content: full-width chrome (join bar, greeting header, Coach/Org mode toggle), a run of notification-style cards (Getting Started, then 7 conditional notice cards, then the Org-mode weekly rollup), and the actual dashboard content (Your Teams strip, the hero, Development Pulse, Upcoming Practices, the bottom action row) -- all textually interleaved in that order, not grouped by what the handoff's spec wanted grouped. Unlike Builder's two-pane split (one contiguous region, one clean cut point), Home's target layout (left: hero + Upcoming Practices; right: Getting Started + notices + Development Pulse) needed pieces that were not adjacent in the source reassembled together.

Solved by extracting every section into its own named `const` (`joinBarContent`, `headerContent`, `modeToggleContent`, `gettingStartedContent`, `noticesContent`, `orgRollupContent`, `yourTeamsContent`, `heroContent`, `devPulseContent`, `upcomingContent`, `bottomRowContent`) in the exact same relative order they already appeared in the file -- since nothing later in that list depends on anything declared after it, this needed zero physical relocation of any block's content, only turning each `{cond && <div>...}` JSX-child expression into a `const x = (cond && <div>...);` statement. The final `return` then assembles them two different ways: at mobile, in the identical order/nesting as before (full-width chrome, then notices, then the single `padding:"0 16px"` column with everything else); at BB, the join bar/header/mode toggle stay full width, Your Teams gets its own full-width row, and `<TwoPane left right/>` takes the rest -- left getting hero + Upcoming Practices + the bottom row, right getting Getting Started + notices + the org rollup + Development Pulse. Every piece is the exact same component instance and props either way, per the pass's own non-negotiable.

Verified live with the QA head-coach account at 1440x900, exactly 1024px, just below it at 1023px (confirmed the fallback to the single-column mobile layout), and 390x844 mobile (pixel-identical to the pre-BB screen, confirmed against a fresh tab with zero console errors). The always-present pieces (header, mode toggle absent since this account has no orgs, Your Teams, the Up Next hero, Development Pulse, Upcoming Practices with all 4 practices including the one built in Builder earlier this session) all rendered correctly in both the two-column BB arrangement and the single mobile column. `npm run build` and the full 53-test `vitest` suite both pass. Not verified: the Getting Started card and the 7 notification cards in their new right-column position, since none were active/pending for the QA fixture account at verification time -- the extraction is mechanical (same component, same props, no logic touched) so risk is low, but flagged honestly in Known Gaps rather than assumed correct. Screens 4 through 10 (Teams, Library, Schedule, Roster, Equipment, Goals & Insights, Practice Detail, Settings) are still queued.

### Forty-eighth session continued a fourth time (2026-08-16): rejected the permission-check-only fix, actually split the overloaded field

Direct feedback, in response to the previous entry's fix: "this isn't how I want this set up. please revert the coach picker to only be who will lead the station. on the top of each block, there should be an option next to choose drill from library to delegate." Correctly rejected the `can_build_practices`-inside-the-RPC fix as papering over the real problem -- reusing `stations.team_staff_id` for two purposes meant a head coach still couldn't look at Builder and tell whether they were setting who leads a station or who designs it, no matter how well-enforced the backend was. Reverted Builder's Coach picker to exactly its pre-feature self (unrestricted, live-leader-only, no editing implication, no explanatory copy needed since there's nothing to explain anymore). Added a new, genuinely separate `stations.delegated_to` column and a "Plan This Station" control in Builder (next to Choose from Library, per the request) that's the actual field `update_station_content`/the assignment notice/`MyStationBuilderScreen` routing all key off now -- plus a live Planned/Needs Planning status per delegated station, visible collapsed and rolled up per block, so a head coach can tell at a glance whether they need to step in themselves. See the Feature Inventory entry above for the full shape and Gotchas for the "why a permission check alone wasn't the real fix" writeup. Verified live: direct RPC scripts confirmed a coach set only as live leader (delegated_to null) is correctly refused, delegated_to alone is sufficient regardless of who's set as leader, and the assignment notice fires on delegated_to changes, not leader changes; a full browser pass confirmed the reverted picker shows every roster coach again, the new delegate picker shows only eligible coaches, and the status badges render correctly both expanded and collapsed. `npm run build` and the full 53-test `vitest` suite both pass.

### Forty-eighth session continued a third time (2026-08-16): closing a real permission gap the Multi-Coach Builder work left open, found from direct feedback

Direct feedback, a genuinely good question: "how is it clear I want them to design that station rather than just be assigned to run what I tell them to run?" Builder's own Coach picker was narrowed to `can_build_practices`-eligible coaches when this feature shipped earlier the same day, but nothing told a head coach that picking someone there now does double duty (design rights, not just live leadership) -- and checking that question surfaced a real, unreported authorization gap underneath it: `update_station_content` never re-checked `can_build_practices`, only "is the caller currently this station's assigned coach," so reassigning a station's *live* leader (a separate, intentionally-open picker in Practice Setup) would silently also grant real editing rights to a coach who was never actually delegated planning permission. See Gotchas for the full writeup. Fixed the RPC itself, suppressed the assignment notice for a non-eligible leader, matched the same check into both client-side routing gates (`BuilderRoute`, `PracticeDetail`), and added explanatory copy at both pickers so the distinction is stated plainly instead of inferred. Verified live: a direct RPC script confirmed a non-delegate assistant set as a station's live leader is correctly refused by `update_station_content` and gets no assignment notice, then confirmed the identical write path succeeds and the notice fires once actually granted `can_build_practices`. `npm run build` and the full 53-test `vitest` suite both pass.

### Forty-eighth session continued (2026-08-16): a real bug, reported separately -- Public Library drills silently failing to save into a practice

Direct report, unrelated to the Multi-Coach Builder work above: a practice built from several Public Library drills plus one own-library drill showed everything correctly in Builder, but "Run Now" only carried the one own-library drill through to Practice Setup. Root-caused (not just fixed on a guess) by actually reproducing it live with the QA head account -- added two real Public Library drills plus one own drill, hit Run Now, and read the browser console during the save: `saveActivityTree insert activity: {code: 42501, ... "new row violates row-level security policy for table practice_activities"}`, twice, once per public-library drill. See Gotchas for the full root cause -- in short, `can_link_drill_to_practice` (the RLS gate practice_activities_insert_manage's `WITH CHECK` calls) never got a public-catalog branch when Public Library shipped, even though the sibling functions that gate *browsing* a drill did, so a drill visible enough to add to a Builder draft wasn't necessarily linkable at save time. Also proactively fixed the same gap in `can_link_drill_to_template`/`_station` (not yet reported, but certain to fail identically) and added the peer-sharing branch those two were missing entirely. `20260816010000_public_catalog_drill_linking_fix.sql`. Verified live end-to-end twice: reproduced the original failure with a fresh practice (console errors + Practice Setup/Edit-Practice both confirmed only 1 of 3 activities actually persisted), applied the fix, then rebuilt the identical practice and confirmed all 3 activities (32 total minutes) now show correctly on Practice Setup with zero console errors. Separately verified peer-shared-library linking with a direct RPC script (a fresh personal drill owned by the assistant account, `set_own_library_share` actually toggled on first) -- confirmed that mechanism already worked correctly once the sharing toggle was genuinely on; the QA fixture team just happened to have it off, a correct denial rather than a second bug. `npm run build` and the full 53-test `vitest` suite both pass.

### Forty-eighth session (2026-08-16): Multi-Coach Builder, implementing a handoff spec in full

Asked to read a handoff doc (`ROP-Multi-Coach-Builder-Handoff.md`) describing a new feature -- a head coach builds a practice's station blocks and assigns each station to a specific rostered coach, who then independently fills in their own station's drills/notes/equipment, safely whether that happens days apart or at the exact same moment as another coach editing a different station -- and build it. The doc was thorough and mostly correct about the live schema (confirmed rather than assumed, per its own instruction): stations are real child rows (`stations` table), not a JSONB array as its own §3 hedged; `stations.team_staff_id` already existed as the coach-assignment field (no new column needed); Builder's own `StationConfig` already had a full pre-live "Assign Leader" pill picker (green/yellow/move-logic) -- the doc's §5 description of this as still-live-only was stale, it just needed eligibility-filtering, not building from scratch; `team_staff.can_build_practices` was exactly the existing delegated-planning permission the doc guessed at. See the new Feature Inventory entry above (Planning & scheduling) for the full shape of what was built -- summarized: the actual concurrency fix is a new `security definer` RPC (`update_station_content`) that does one atomic per-station `UPDATE`, re-deriving the caller's real authorization fresh from the DB on every call rather than trusting the client, replacing what would otherwise be a `savePracticeTree` full-tree overwrite for this specific edit; a non-manager coach with a station assigned lands on a new restricted `MyStationBuilderScreen` instead of the normal skeleton editor, with read-only summary cards for sibling stations and a real Home notification (`station_assignment_notices`, DB-trigger-populated so it can't miss a write path) since assignment and editing happen on decoupled schedules; presence and a per-block completion indicator round out the head coach's own view; the one-per-team practice-delegate cap is gone (Pro+ now unlimited, matching the doc's entitlements section) since station-level eligibility reuses that same permission. One real bug found and fixed live during verification, not requested and not pre-existing -- see Gotchas (the completion indicator's first version miscounted an untouched station as "filled in" because of a placeholder-name collision that survives a save). Verified live end-to-end with the persistent QA accounts: a direct RPC/RLS script proved the core concurrency claim (two coaches' simultaneous writes to different stations in the same block both land, neither clobbers the other, unauthorized/stale writes fail with distinct client-matchable errors for both the reassignment and deletion edge cases) before ever touching a browser, then a full sequential-identity browser pass (this file's own documented workaround for two disposable sessions sharing one origin's `localStorage`) confirmed every UI piece -- the filtered Assign Leader picker, the completion indicator (after the fix), the restricted editor's save/attribution/presence/summary-cards, the Home notification's full lifecycle, and `PracticeDetail`'s conditional entry point both present and correctly absent. `npm run build` and the full 53-test `vitest` suite both pass. Out of scope, matching the handoff's own explicit exclusions: real-time (sub-poll) visibility into another coach's typing, any CRDT/OT merge logic, and skeleton-level concurrent editing.

### Forty-seventh session (2026-08-13), same day: live co-testing as a head coach and an assistant surfaced two real RLS gaps, a real attendance-sync gap, and several styling/parity fixes

Direct feedback from actually running the forty-sixth session's work live with two real coaches on the same practice, not synthetic single-account testing. Two categories of finding: real bugs traced to root cause (not glossed over), and direct styling/wording requests.

**Two RLS visibility gaps, found by testing the assistant's own real token directly against PostgREST rather than guessing from code.** `assets_select_access` and `locations_select_access` were both `can_access_owned`-only, with no branch for "referenced by a practice on a team I coach" -- correct for a drill-library context, silently wrong for a live-practice one. This explained several reports at once that looked unrelated on the surface: the assistant's Equipment Needed section and every per-drill/station equipment pill being empty, and the transition screen's new area summary reading "Station 1 to Station 2" for the assistant where the head coach saw real area names, for the *same* practice at the *same* moment. Fixed at the function level (`can_access_location`, so `sublocations_select_access` inherited the fix transitively) and via two new `exists` branches on `assets_select_access`, both keyed off the already-correct `can_access_practice_activity`/`can_access_station`/`can_access_team` checks their own join tables use. See Gotchas for the generalized lesson and what's worth auditing next.

**Practice Setup's attendance never actually synced between coaches -- confirmed the real bug, not assumed it**: present/absent toggles and "I'm here" coach presence were pure local `useState`, never written anywhere, the whole time this screen existed. Modeled as two new array columns directly on `practice_live_sessions` (an ephemeral draft, not the append-only `session_attendance` history table) so every toggle rides the exact sync machinery every other live-session field already uses. Individual taps go through a new atomic RPC (`toggle_setup_presence`) rather than the normal version-checked `writeSession` path specifically to avoid a genuine two-coach concurrent-edit race that a version check would silently resolve by discarding one coach's edit -- the exact "why didn't it update" symptom, just relocated rather than fixed. Verified live: a toggle from one identity appeared on the other's already-open screen within a couple seconds, no reload.

**A coach's screen didn't auto-join the live view when a different coach started the practice**: `stage` (attend vs. live) was only ever resolved once, at mount -- session state itself already synced, nothing was reactively watching it. Fixed with a new effect; verified by flipping `setup_confirmed_at` from outside the coach's own open tab and watching it jump straight into the live view unattended.

**Direct-feedback/styling batch, same session**: Pre-Practice Warmup reworked from an opt-in Practice Components tile into an always-present, collapsed-by-default card (caret, no "Remove") with reworded copy; the timer's subtitle wording tightened; coach-name labels made consistently pill-styled ("Coach: Name") for every viewer instead of only whoever holds control; Up Next expanded to show every remaining activity (scrollable) instead of capping at 1-3; Introduce Stations' equipment badge recolored to match the solid-amber `EquipPill` treatment instead of outlined-yellow-on-white; the transition-screen area summary (new forty-sixth session) made bold and larger; and the station block's own name (new forty-sixth session) extended to `HelperView`'s block-stats line, which hadn't gotten it originally.

Verified: `npm run build` and the full `vitest` suite (53 tests) both pass. Live-verified end-to-end against the persistent QA accounts -- the two RLS fixes confirmed via the assistant's own real access token directly against PostgREST (empty result before, the real row after, for both `assets` and `locations`); the attendance-sync and auto-join fixes confirmed by driving the session's actual server state as the assistant identity (real RPC calls, real writes) while watching the head coach's own already-open browser tab pick up each change live; every styling fix confirmed visually in-browser. Two disposable test live-sessions created during this verification were explicitly marked `abandoned` afterward, not left dangling.

### Forty-seventh session continued, third pass (2026-08-14): a drill's ellipsis menu clipped at the bottom of the Library list, swept to every other list-row menu and autocomplete popup in the app

Direct feedback: viewing a drill near the bottom of the Library list, its ellipsis menu was blocked by the bottom of the screen and the tab bar. Root cause: `.mini-menu`'s own CSS (`App.jsx`) always opens downward -- `top: calc(100% - 4px)` -- with no check for whether there's actually room below the button. A row near the bottom of any scrollable list has none, so the menu renders half (or fully) off the visible viewport, further covered by the fixed bottom tab bar.

Fixed the reported drill row first (`NewLibraryScreen.jsx`), then, per direct instruction to sweep related issues before committing, audited every other `.mini-menu` render site in the codebase -- 21 in total. Extracted the fix into one shared helper, `menuNeedsToOpenUpward(rect, thresholdPx)` (`constants.js`): measures the trigger's own `getBoundingClientRect()` against `window.innerHeight` at open time and returns whether there's enough room below for the menu's own worst-case height. 14 sites are genuine instances of the same bug -- every one gets a companion `*Up` boolean state alongside its existing "which row is open" state, set on click/keystroke, consumed as a conditional `top:"auto",bottom:"calc(100% - 4px)"` override:

- **List-row ellipsis menus**: Library's drill row (`NewLibraryScreen.jsx`, the one actually reported), equipment row, location row, and template row; Builder's own stale-drill-warning menu (`App.jsx`) and `TemplateWorkspace`'s identical copy (`NewLibraryScreen.jsx`); the roster's player/coach/pending-invite row menus (`App.jsx`, three sites sharing one `openMenu` state); an org's member-list row menu (`TeamsListScreen.jsx`); Home's upcoming-practices row menu (`HomeScreen.jsx`); the public content catalog's admin-only drill row menu (`PublicLibraryScreen.jsx`).
- **@mention autocomplete popups** (same underlying `.mini-menu` CSS, same failure mode, just keystroke-triggered instead of click-triggered): `HelperView`'s standalone `NoteComposer` card and the coach's own end-of-practice note textarea (`CommandScreen.jsx`) -- both can end up anywhere on a scrollable page depending on content/scroll position, so both needed the real dynamic check.

7 sites were deliberately left untouched, for two different reasons. Four are single-instance menus fixed to a screen header/toolbar (the team workspace's own ellipsis, Home's help button, the live view's own header ellipsis in both viewer types, the roster's sort-order dropdown) -- always near the top of the screen by construction, never at risk of the bottom-clipping failure mode. One (the live "quick note" bar's own @mention popup, `CommandScreen.jsx`) was already fixed correctly in an earlier session with a **hardcoded** upward direction, not a dynamic check -- deliberately left as-is rather than switched to the new dynamic helper, since that bar is *always* pinned to the fixed bottom of the screen by design, and swapping in a threshold-based check there would only add regression risk for zero benefit. `PublicLibraryScreen`'s own light-theme in-drill equipment display and the `content_catalogs`-only drill picker were not menus at all, just similarly-named unrelated UI, and weren't touched.

Verified: `npm run build` and the full `vitest` suite (53 tests) both pass. Live-verified the mechanism itself two ways against the QA account -- a normal desktop-height viewport renders every menu identically to before (no `style` override, confirmed via `getComputedStyle`/`getAttribute('style')` on the drill row, the equipment row, and a roster player row), and a deliberately short viewport (400x400) correctly flips the roster's own last-row menu upward with its full rect (`top`/`bottom`) landing inside the visible viewport instead of past its bottom edge.

### Forty-seventh session continued, second pass (2026-08-14): Introduce Stations' equipment pill was still white on the coach's own view, equipment/player gear had never actually been split anywhere, and block-intro now auto-advances like the transition screen already did

Three items, the first two both scoped to "Introduce Stations" but requiring a wider fix once traced. **The white-pill fix from the prior pass only actually landed on `HelperView`.** Root cause: that fix searched for the literal string `st.equipment.join(", ")` inside the white-pill JSX pattern with `replace_all`, assuming it existed identically in both viewers -- the coach's own block-intro used a differently-named local (`stEquip`, resolved from Builder-picker ids via `data.assets`), so it was never matched and stayed on the original outlined-yellow-on-white style. Confirmed via direct `getComputedStyle` inspection (`background: rgb(255, 255, 255)`) before touching any code, not just a re-read of the diff.

**Player gear had never actually been separated from equipment anywhere in the live display layer**, despite `assets.type` (`team_equipment`/`player_gear`) existing since the asset picker was built -- every display site merged both types into one flat "Equipment" list, and the free-text `playerGear` string field several sites also checked was fully dead code (`mapActivityRow` hardcodes it to `''` for stations, `saveActivityTree` never persists it). Fixed at both layers: `get_live_session_view` (`20260814000000_live_session_equipment_split_by_type.sql`) now returns `equipment`/`player_gear` as two separate arrays of `{name,acquired}` objects instead of one flat name-string array, at every level the RPC touches (current activity, stations, upcoming_activities, all_activities) -- a genuine breaking change to the RPC's shape, so every `HelperView` site consuming `.equipment` needed updating, not just the one the feedback named. Coach-side sites needed no RPC change (they already resolve equipment locally via `data.assets`, which already carries `.type`); added `splitEquipFor` next to the existing `equipNamesFor` to partition by type. A new shared `EquipGearRow`/`PlayerGearPill` pair (next to the existing `EquipPill`) renders both groups consistently -- labeled "Equipment"/"Player Gear", amber vs. orange, both acquired-aware -- reused across all 8 equipment-display sites in both viewers (top-of-live plain drill, block-intro, rotation-focus detail, all-stations tap list). `PreviewView`/`get_preview_view` (the separate pre-live anonymous preview screen) was deliberately left untouched -- out of scope, already object-shaped and not what was reported broken.

**Block-intro now auto-advances into Rotation 1 exactly like the transition screen already auto-advances into the next rotation** -- direct feedback the coach shouldn't hear the buzzer and have to tap Next just to start the first station timer. `advance()`'s own `inBlockIntro` branch already did the right thing (the same call `startBlock()` makes); added a second effect alongside the existing transition auto-advance, sharing its `transitionAdvancedRef` guard since the two phases are mutually exclusive and `transitionTo` already resets it on every real phase change either could fire from. Extended the buzzer-suppression effect's existing `!inTrans` guard to `!inTrans&&!inBlockIntro` so block-intro stays silent the same way.

Verified live against the persistent QA team/practice with a real two-typed-equipment station (Cones/team, Pinnies/player, both acquired) and one deliberately-not-acquired asset available for the red-pill path: dry-ran the RPC migration in a rolled-back transaction before applying, then confirmed in-browser via `getComputedStyle` that both pills are no longer white and render in their intended colors, on both the coach's own tab and a real `HelperView` tab from a freshly-created share token. For the auto-advance, reloaded a live QA session that was already sitting well past its block-intro's own timer -- it auto-advanced immediately on load with no tap, the session row's `in_block_intro` flipped to `false` server-side, and a separate already-open `HelperView` tab picked up "Rotation 1 of 2" within its normal poll interval with no action taken there at all. Test session marked `completed` and its temporary share token deleted afterward, not left dangling.

### Forty-seventh session continued (2026-08-13), same day: two small direct-feedback follow-ups from re-reading the just-shipped work

Two quick corrections, both confirmed live in-browser (a fresh station block, with an activity added after it specifically to exercise the fix). **Up Next inside a station block was still showing "Station rotation coming up" instead of the real answer**: direct feedback it should show what's next *after* the block finishes, not the next rotation. Removed the mid-block special case entirely -- `liveActs.slice(idx+1)`/`session.upcoming_activities` already meant "after the current top-level activity" regardless of which rotation is showing, so the placeholder (and its orphaned "See next transition" local preview, `previewTrans` state and modal, now deleted rather than left dead) was hiding an answer the data already had. **The coach-name pill fix from earlier today left a redundant standalone "Coach" label in front of the pill on Practice Setup's own rows** (the pill itself already reads "Coach: Name") -- removed both occurrences (`SetupActivityRow`, `SetupStationBlockRow`). Verified: build and the full test suite pass; both fixes confirmed visually in-browser, including `HelperView`'s own Up Next picking up the same "what's after the block" answer.

### Forty-sixth session (2026-08-13): Pre-Practice Warmup, Equipment Needed grouped by Area, named station blocks, a transition-screen rotation summary + tap-to-see Player Focus, and a self-advancing transition timer

A seven-part enhancement request for the pre-practice/live-station experience, all direct spec from Jax rather than reactive feedback. Given the size and an explicit "get this right the first time, ask if unsure," two clarifying questions were asked before writing any code (see the conversation, not reproduced here): whether "Pre-Practice Warmup" should be modeled as a real activity-tree item (pinned/excluded everywhere) or a plain field on the practice itself, and whether to build all seven pieces in one pass with a single verification pass at the end, or one at a time. Jax chose the plain-field model (recommended -- structurally guarantees it can't affect duration/drill-count/skill-tags/the live rotation state machine, since it isn't an activity at all) and one continuous pass with thorough live verification at the end.

**Equipment Needed, grouped by Area instead of coach name, two columns when there's more than one area.** New `groupEquipmentByArea` (`constants.js`), a new shared `EquipmentByArea` grid component used by both `PracticeSetupScreen` and the anonymous `/preview/:token` `PreviewView`. See Live sessions above for detail.

**Pre-Practice Warmup**: new `pre_practice_notes text` column on `practices`/`templates` (`20260813150000_pre_practice_notes.sql`, plus `get_preview_view` extended in `20260813151000_preview_view_pre_practice_notes.sql` so the anonymous preview screen gets it too) -- deliberately not an activity, per the design decision above. A new tile in Builder's Practice Components grid reveals a pinned, visually distinct (dashed border, light-green fill) card at the top of the Run of Practice list, outside the drag context entirely; shown on Practice Setup under the countdown timer, above Attendance. Threaded through every save/duplicate call site that touches a practice's activities (see Planning & scheduling above for the full list, including a real would-be regression caught before it shipped: the mid-live "Edit Practice" save path would have silently wiped it on every save, since an omitted field writes as `null`).

**Station blocks can be named.** Turned out no migration was needed at all -- `practice_activities.name` already round-tripped for every activity type, including `station_block`; nothing ever set it and every display site just hardcoded the string "Station Block" instead of reading it. Added a "Block Name" field in Builder and swapped every hardcoded occurrence for `act.name||"Station Block"`. The live view's "Block 1 of 2" prefix (shown only when a practice has more than one station block) is removed outright, per direct feedback to just leave it out rather than replace it with anything.

**Transition screen**: a new one-line rotation summary ("Infield to East Cage, East Cage to West Cage, ... Outfield to Infield") ahead of the existing per-group TO:/FROM: cards, derived from the block's own fixed station order -- present in both the coach's live view and `HelperView`. Each card also gained a "Player Focus" button when the destination station's drill has a tagged skill category, opening the same Player Focus modal pattern a plain drill already used, scoped to the specific incoming group. **Real gotcha hit and documented in Live sessions above, not an app bug**: test data created via direct SQL used a skill tag scoped to a different coach account than the QA test account, so RLS correctly hid it from `data.skillTags` while `drill_tags` (scoped to the drill's own ownership) still referenced it -- looked exactly like a broken join until traced.

**Self-advancing transition timer**: the transition screen no longer needs a manual "Next" tap once its own timer expires -- it silently advances into the next rotation and that group's timer. Gated on `isController` so only the controlling coach's client ever writes (every other viewer just observes the resulting update through the sync machinery this app already built for exactly this purpose), with a one-shot guard ref reset everywhere the existing buzz/warn guards already are. The normal time's-up buzzer is suppressed specifically for this one phase (the silent auto-advance is the intended signal); every other phase still buzzes and waits as before. Confirmed live, unattended, three separate times across two test practices, with a second browser tab on a real `/live/:token` helper link watching in parallel the whole time and staying correctly in sync (rotated groups, no independent action) via its existing 3-second poll -- see Live sessions above for the full mechanism.

Also confirmed (no code change needed): default audio-on-off state already matches for a head coach, an assistant coach (literally the same component, no role branching), and a helper view (same component whether the share link's holder is rostered or fully anonymous).

Verified: `npm run build` and the full `vitest` suite (53 tests) both pass. Live-verified end-to-end against the persistent QA Head account (`ropqa-head@example.com`) and a real anonymous `/live/:token` tab side by side -- built and ran three real practices covering every piece above, including deliberately shortening station/transition durations to observe multiple real auto-advance cycles rather than trusting the code alone. Left the QA drill's added skill tag + a player focus note as reusable fixtures for a future session, per this file's own "feel free to add more" convention, rather than tearing them down.

### Forty-fifth session (2026-08-10): live-session sync reliability, Up Next station blanking, and a portrait-only lock

Direct feedback from the app's first live multi-device practice test: a head coach (Mike) and an assistant (Jax), plus six anonymous helpers, on the venue's admittedly weak wifi/cellular. Symptoms reported, deliberately more specific on a follow-up prompt than the first: not just clock drift, but one screen advancing to a different drill or station rotation than another and the two never converging on their own, the timer differing even between two viewers on the same station, "Take Control" appearing to do nothing on the first tap before working on a second, and "Up Next" for a station block going blank (a recurrence of something seen in an earlier test).

Root-caused to `CommandScreen`'s `session` state depending entirely on a single Supabase Realtime `postgres_changes` subscription with no reconciliation backstop of any kind. Realtime has no catch-up/replay -- an update that happens while a client's websocket is disconnected is gone for good the instant it reconnects, and nothing in the app ever re-fetched to catch up. Whoever happened to hold control always looked correct only because their own writes updated local state directly and immediately; everyone else was purely dependent on realtime delivery, which a flaky field connection can't guarantee. This also explains the Take Control double-tap: the version-conflict safety check silently no-ops into a resync (rather than an error) when local state is stale, so the first tap "fixed" the screen without granting control, and the second tap (now caught up) worked.

**Fixed with two backstops in `CommandScreen.jsx`**: `subscribeToLiveSession` (`src/supabase.js`) now reports its own connection status; the client forces a full reconcile on every reconnect, not just the first, plus runs a 7-second background poll as a second backstop for a socket that looks connected but silently isn't. Both funnel through one `reconcileSession` that only ever moves state forward (keyed off `version`) and never treats a transient fetch failure as "the session ended."

**Separately root-caused "Up Next in stations went blank"**: `fetchLatestGroups` (feeding the station-rotation view) only handled a clean Postgrest `{error}`, not a genuine network-level throw -- on bad venue wifi it could reject outright, and neither of its two calling effects (station-block groupings, per-station sub-groupings) had a try/catch around their own async IIFE, so an unhandled rejection silently left that data `null` for the rest of the mount with nothing to ever retry it. Hardened the fetch function itself against throwing and gave both effects a bounded 3-attempt retry.

**Portrait lock, per direct feedback that the screen should never rotate to landscape**: added `"orientation":"portrait"` to `manifest.json` (covers an installed Android/Chrome PWA) plus a pure-CSS full-screen "rotate back to portrait" overlay in `index.html`, scoped to touch/coarse-pointer devices in landscape, as the iOS fallback (Safari has never honored the manifest orientation member or the Screen Orientation lock API).

Verified: `npm run build` and the full `vitest` suite (53 tests) both pass; the rotate-lock overlay was confirmed live in-browser at real device breakpoints. **Not yet live-verified with two real devices under real flaky-network conditions** -- confirm the actual desync/blank/double-tap symptoms are gone at the next live test.

### Forty-fifth session continued (2026-08-10): timer-control jolt, audio reliability, and rolling the portrait lock back off iOS

Same-day follow-up, asked for directly after the above: "the bread and butter of this app is currently the weakest part... how do we make it better," naming two concrete symptoms -- audio inconsistency during the run, and the ±1 min timer buttons feeling delayed and jolty -- plus a decision to reverse part of the portrait-lock work from earlier the same day.

**±1 min jolt, the write-side twin of the Take Control bug fixed earlier the same day.** `writeSession` read `session.version` from whichever render was current when it was called, not when the request actually fired -- two rapid writes (±1 tapped twice quickly, a nudge right after a pause) could race, the loser get rejected by the server as a stale-version conflict, and have its own optimistic update silently discarded by the resync that follows. Visibly: the timer jumps forward on the tap, then snaps back a beat later. Fixed by funneling every live-session write through a queue (`writeQueueRef`, a chained promise) so a queued write only actually executes once every write ahead of it has resolved, reading `sessionRef.current` -- now kept authoritative the instant a write resolves, not just via the passive mirror effect, which lags a render cycle behind -- instead of a stale closure.

**Audio inconsistency, two separate real causes, not general flakiness.** The "two minutes remaining" cue depended on `elapsed` landing inside a narrow 4-second window ([118,122]s remaining) on a live tick, but the `setInterval` driving `elapsed` is throttled or fully suspended by mobile browsers whenever the tab is briefly backgrounded (an app switch, a notification) -- a throttled tick can jump `elapsed` straight over that whole window in one step, silently skipping the cue for the rest of the phase with zero trace it happened. Widened to an at-or-past-threshold check (`rem2<=120&&rem2>0`), the same robust shape the time's-up buzzer effect already used (`elapsed>=phaseSecs`) for the identical reason. Separately, and more seriously: every sound-file `audio.play()` call in the live view swallowed a rejected promise with `.catch(()=>{})` and nothing else happening -- an interrupted audio session (a phone call, a Bluetooth handoff, autoplay policy re-engaging after backgrounding, a `.wav` that hadn't finished loading on the field's own connection) meant the time's-up cue's chained `audio.onended -> speak("Time")` never fired at all, going completely silent for the single most consequential cue in the app (the one marking a drill actually over) with nothing to show for it. Every failure path there (`play()` rejection, `onerror`, a missing audio element, a thrown exception) now falls back to speaking "Time" directly, since `speechSynthesis` is a separate playback pathway from `<audio>` and in practice often still works when the audio element doesn't. Also added an immediate forced `setNow(Date.now())` on `visibilitychange` returning to visible (alongside the existing Wake-Lock re-request there), so a cue that came due while the tab was backgrounded fires the instant the coach returns instead of waiting out whatever's left on a throttled interval.

**Portrait lock rolled back for iOS specifically, direct feedback: "the black screen is useless, but at least the awkward landscape screen is still usable."** The CSS rotate-lock overlay shipped earlier the same day was meant as the iOS fallback (since iOS ignores the manifest orientation member entirely), but in practice a full-screen black block is strictly worse than the layout it replaces -- iOS has no way to actually force the device back to portrait, only to hide the app until the coach does it themselves. Rolled back to iOS only, via the standard `@supports (-webkit-touch-callout:none)` CSS-only WebKit-detection trick (only WebKit, and therefore only iOS's browsers, recognizes that property as supported at all): the overlay still covers Android/other touch devices, where it remains a real improvement (the manifest lock only applies to an installed PWA, not a plain browser tab), but is explicitly forced off on iOS. Confirmed logically correct against a Chromium-based browser tool (`CSS.supports('-webkit-touch-callout','none')` correctly evaluates `false` there, same as real Android, so the override doesn't fire and the base rule still shows the overlay) -- not verified against a real iOS device, since this project has no access to actual Safari/WebKit to test against.

Verified: `npm run build` and the full `vitest` suite (53 tests) pass. The write-queue and audio-threshold changes are timing/race-condition fixes with no new UI surface of their own to screenshot -- verified by reading the exact race through rather than a live two-device repro (same caveat noted in the entry above). One known, accepted gap: neither this fix nor the sync fix above can survive a *sustained* backgrounding (minutes, not moments) -- Wake Lock and the silent-audio-loop trick protect against the screen locking, not against someone genuinely switching away from the app for a while, which is a real platform ceiling, not something client-side code can fully solve.

### Forty-fifth session continued again (2026-08-10): portrait lock pulled entirely, deferred to a real design pass

Direct follow-up to the iOS-only rollback above: rather than leave an Android-only partial version live, pulled the portrait lock out completely for now -- both `manifest.json`'s `"orientation":"portrait"` and the CSS rotate-lock overlay in `index.html` (including its now-unused `@supports (-webkit-touch-callout:none)` iOS carve-out) are gone, `index.html` back to its pre-lock state. No screens rotate-block on any platform right now; a device in landscape just shows the app's normal (not landscape-optimized) layout, same as before any of this was attempted today.

Logged as a real Known Gap rather than just reverted silently, since the underlying constraint that made the iOS attempt land badly is still true and worth knowing before trying again: iOS has no web API that can actually force a device back to portrait, only ways to hide the app until the person rotates it themselves, so "block the screen until they rotate" is the only lock *available* on iOS, not a bug in how it was built -- a future attempt needs to either accept that tradeoff deliberately (unlike this attempt, where feedback was that it wasn't worth it) or design the live view to work in both orientations instead. Verified: `npm run build` and the full `vitest` suite (53 tests) pass; no other code touched this pass.

### Forty-fifth session continued a fourth time (2026-08-10): a peace-of-mind sync badge and manual refresh

Direct follow-up: "I don't want to add a refresh button with the expectation that people will need to use it, but if I notice my screen isn't loading, it would be nice to have a refresh option... can the same button display some kind of confidence indicator that we're live and connected... then maybe something that would indicate there is a chance the data is stale."

Answered honestly rather than just building what was asked: the reconciliation fixes from earlier the same day handle *stale-but-eventually-self-healing* data well (within ~7-25s), but can't help if the tab's own JS is genuinely stuck -- a different failure class a manual escape hatch is still worth having for. Rather than write new "soft resync" logic, leaned on something already true and already relied on elsewhere in this app: a live session's entire state (timer, stage, controller) already re-derives from the server on every fresh mount, so a real `window.location.reload()` is already a fully correct resync for free, and unlike any in-app retry it still works if the running JS itself is the thing that's stuck.

**Built**: `SyncBadge` (`CommandScreen.jsx`), replacing the header's previously-decorative static "● Live" label. `lastSyncedAtRef` stamps `Date.now()` on every real confirmation of currency -- a realtime message, a successful reconcile poll, a successful write -- so "seconds since last sync" is one honest signal regardless of which path is actually working at a given moment. Three states, purely time-based (not tied to the realtime channel's own connection status, so a harmless brief reconnect doesn't flash a false alarm): green "Live" (<12s), amber "Reconnecting…" (12-25s), red "Refresh" (>25s) -- always tappable at every level, always triggers a real reload.

Verified live against the persistent QA team/practice (see Working Conventions): built a one-drill practice, ran it, confirmed the badge renders correctly in its healthy state (green dot, "Live" label, a real `<button>` with a "Tap to refresh" title), tapped it mid-drill, and confirmed a genuine page reload occurred *and* landed back in the exact same live session with the timer correctly picking up from server state -- directly validating the "reload is already a correct resync" design assumption, not just asserting it. Cleaned up afterward via Abort Practice, per this file's own no-stray-test-data convention. Did not verify the amber/red degraded states live (would need an actual sustained connectivity gap); the 12s/25s thresholds are a reasoned default against the existing 7s poll interval, not empirically tuned -- worth revisiting if they turn out too twitchy or too slow in real conditions.

### Forty-fourth session (2026-08-10): drill/equipment redesign, per-coach-pair library sharing, several assistant-coach gating fixes, a consultation-lead notification pipeline, and three live-session fixes
